=== [0:21] 왜 안 자고 있는 사람들 뭐야? ===
meaning: What's with all you people who aren't sleeping?
tone: Informal, curious, slightly teasing. Addressing her fans in the chat.
--- word ---
term: 왜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: why
notes: A fundamental question word. In this context, it's used to express surprise, similar to 'How come...?' or 'What's the deal with...?'. It's a common way to start a casual, rhetorical question.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: This is a negative adverb placed before a verb or adjective to negate it. It implies the subject's choice or will. This is different from 못, which implies inability ('cannot'). For example, '안 자요' (I'm not sleeping) vs. '못 자요' (I can't sleep).
--- word ---
term: 자고
type: Verb
breakdown: 자다 + -고
meaning: sleeping and...
notes: 자다 is the verb 'to sleep'. The -고 ending connects it to the auxiliary verb 있다 to form the present progressive tense, 'to be sleeping'.
--- word ---
term: 있는
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다 + -는
meaning: who are / that are
notes: 있다 means 'to be'. The -는 ending turns the verb phrase '자고 있다' (to be sleeping) into an adjective that can modify a noun. It describes the current, ongoing state of the noun that follows.
--- word ---
term: 사람들
type: Noun
breakdown: 사람 + -들
meaning: people
notes: 사람 means 'person'. The suffix -들 is a plural marker. While not always necessary for plural nouns in Korean, it's used here for emphasis, highlighting the number of fans who are awake.
--- word ---
term: 뭐야?
type: Pronoun + Verb Ending
breakdown: 뭐 + -이야
meaning: what is it?
notes: A very common informal question. It's a contraction of 뭐 (what) + 이야 (is it?). Here, it's used colloquially to mean 'What's up with...?' or 'Who are...?', expressing surprise at the situation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고 있다
meaning: Present progressive tense, equivalent to 'be V-ing' in English. It describes an ongoing action or state.
example: 지금 밥을 먹고 있어요. (I am eating right now.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 Noun
meaning: A way to modify a noun with an action verb in the present tense. It creates a descriptive phrase like 'the [Noun] who is [V-ing]'.
example: 노래하는 사람 (a person who is singing)
level: Beginner

=== [0:27] 또 이 시간에 왔대 ===
meaning: (She) came at this hour again, they say.
tone: Self-referential, playful. Quoting what she imagines her fans are thinking about her.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: again
notes: Indicates the repetition of an action. It's similar to 다시 (again), but 또 often carries a nuance of 'yet again' or can imply a slight feeling of surprise or annoyance at the repetition.
--- word ---
term: 이
type: Determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: this
notes: Used before a noun to specify something close to the speaker. The set is 이 (this), 그 (that, near the listener), and 저 (that, over there).
--- word ---
term: 시간에
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 시간 + -에
meaning: at this time/hour
notes: 시간 means 'time'. The particle -에 is attached to nouns of time and place to mean 'at', 'in', or 'on'.
--- word ---
term: 왔대
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + -았- + -대
meaning: came, they say
notes: -대 is a very common colloquial contraction of the indirect quotation form -다고 해, meaning 'they say that...' or 'I heard that...'. Here, the speaker is playfully quoting the imagined thoughts of her fans ('Oh, she came on live again at this hour').
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(ㄴ/는)대
meaning: A casual indirect quotation ending, short for -(ㄴ/는)다고 해. It's used to report something you heard from someone else. In this case, it's used creatively to voice what she thinks her fans are saying.
example: 친구가 오늘 바쁘대. (My friend says she's busy today.)
level: Intermediate

=== [0:34] 그냥 심심해서 ===
meaning: Just because I was bored.
tone: Informal, explanatory, trailing off. Answering the implied question of why she started the live stream.
--- word ---
term: 그냥
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just / for no particular reason
notes: A very versatile adverb used when there's no special reason for an action. It can mean 'just', 'as it is', or 'without any specific purpose'. It's extremely common in daily conversation.
--- word ---
term: 심심해서
type: Adjective
breakdown: 심심하다 + -아/어서
meaning: because I was bored
notes: 심심하다 means 'to be bored'. The -아/어서 ending is attached to verbs and adjectives to indicate a cause or reason. The following clause (e.g., 'I started the live stream') is omitted as it's clear from the context.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/Adj-아/어서
meaning: A clause connector that means 'because' or 'so'. It links a reason (first clause) to a result (second clause). It's very common for the second clause to be omitted in conversation if the result is obvious.
example: 피곤해서 일찍 잤어요. (I was tired, so I went to bed early.)
level: Beginner

=== [0:40] 왜 안 자고 있어 다들? ===
meaning: Why are you all still awake?
tone: Informal, direct question, friendly.
--- word ---
term: 왜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: why
notes: The standard question word for asking for a reason.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: Negative adverb placed before a verb. '안 자다' means 'to not sleep' by choice, which fits the context of her fans choosing to watch her stream.
--- word ---
term: 자고
type: Verb
breakdown: 자다 + -고
meaning: sleeping and...
notes: The verb 'to sleep' (자다) connected to the auxiliary verb 있다 to form the present progressive.
--- word ---
term: 있어
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다
meaning: are
notes: The informal, non-polite form of 있어요. The full phrase '자고 있어' means 'are sleeping'. Used because she is speaking in 반말 (banmal) to her fans.
--- word ---
term: 다들?
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 다 + -들
meaning: everyone? / you all?
notes: 다 means 'all'. The plural marker -들 is added to create a friendly, collective term of address. It's a very common and natural way to address a group of friends or peers.

=== [0:42] 진짜 이상하다 ===
meaning: It's really strange.
tone: Informal, musing, expressing mild surprise to herself.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really / truly
notes: A very common adverb used to add emphasis. In colloquial speech, 진짜 is often preferred over the slightly more formal 정말 (jeongmal). It can also be used as a standalone exclamation: '진짜?!' (Really?!).
--- word ---
term: 이상하다
type: Adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: is strange / is weird
notes: The dictionary form (or 'plain form') of the adjective. Using the -다 ending like this in speech creates a monologue-like or exclamatory tone, as if the speaker is talking to themselves or making an observation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Plain Form (-다) in Speech
meaning: Using the dictionary form of a verb or adjective as a sentence ender. In conversation, this creates a casual, monologue-like tone. It's used for exclamations, self-talk, or stating a fact to no one in particular.
example: 와, 날씨 좋다! (Wow, the weather is nice!)
level: Intermediate

=== [0:44] 왜 안 자고 있어? ===
meaning: Why are you not sleeping?
tone: Informal, repetitive question, genuinely curious.
--- word ---
term: 왜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: why
notes: Repeating the question for emphasis, showing her genuine curiosity about why so many fans are awake.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: The negative adverb, implying the fans are choosing not to sleep.
--- word ---
term: 자고
type: Verb
breakdown: 자다 + -고
meaning: sleeping and...
notes: Part of the present progressive V-고 있다 construction.
--- word ---
term: 있어?
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다
meaning: are?
notes: The informal question form of 있다. In 반말 (banmal), a statement can be turned into a question simply by raising the intonation at the end.

=== [0:57] 나라 지키느라 못 자고 있구나? ===
meaning: Oh, so you can't sleep because you're defending the country?
tone: Informal, teasing, playful assumption. A common joke in Korea.
--- word ---
term: 나라
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: country / nation
notes: Refers to one's own country. The phrase '나라를 지키다' (to protect the country) is strongly associated with military service.
--- word ---
term: 지키느라
type: Verb
breakdown: 지키다 + -느라
meaning: because of defending
notes: 지키다 means 'to protect/guard'. The grammar pattern -느라(고) indicates that the action in the first clause is the reason for the (often negative) result in the second. It implies effort or being occupied with the first action.
--- word ---
term: 못
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: cannot
notes: A negative adverb indicating inability or impossibility, as opposed to 안 which indicates unwillingness. Here, it implies the fans 'can't' sleep because they are busy with an important task.
--- word ---
term: 자고
type: Verb
breakdown: 자다 + -고
meaning: sleeping and...
notes: Part of the present progressive form.
--- word ---
term: 있구나?
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다 + -구나
meaning: oh, so you are...!
notes: The ending -구나 expresses realization or mild surprise upon learning a new fact. It's like saying 'Oh, so that's why!' or 'I see now that...'. The question mark reflects a rising, playful intonation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-느라(고)
meaning: Indicates that the action in the first clause is the reason for a (usually negative) outcome in the second. It implies being busy or occupied with the first action, which prevents or causes the second. The subject of both clauses must be the same.
example: 숙제하느라 전화를 못 받았어요. (I couldn't answer the phone because I was doing homework.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/Adj-구나
meaning: An exclamatory ending used when the speaker realizes or learns something new. It expresses mild surprise or a new understanding. It's a common feature of casual, reactive speech.
example: 아, 이 사람이 그 배우구나! (Oh, so this person is that actor!)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:06] 우리만 이상하니? ===
meaning: Are we the only weird ones?
tone: Informal, rhetorical question, creating camaraderie with her fans.
--- word ---
term: 우리만
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 우리 + -만
meaning: only us
notes: 우리 means 'we/us'. The particle -만 means 'only' or 'just'. It restricts the scope of the statement to the noun it's attached to. Here, she's including herself and her fans in the group '우리'.
--- word ---
term: 이상하니?
type: Adjective
breakdown: 이상하다 + -니?
meaning: is it strange?
notes: 이상하다 means 'to be strange'. The -니? ending is an informal question form used in 반말 (banmal). It often has a softer, more gentle, or intimate feel compared to other informal endings like -아/어?.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + -만
meaning: A particle meaning 'only' or 'just'. It is attached to a noun to indicate that the description or action applies exclusively to that noun.
example: 오늘만 쉬어요. (I'm resting only today.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/Adj-니?
meaning: A casual question ending used in informal speech (반말). It's often used when speaking to someone of the same age or younger and can convey a soft, friendly, or curious tone.
example: 지금 뭐 하니? (What are you doing now?)
level: Beginner

=== [1:11] 자나가 깼는데 알람이 왔어? ===
meaning: You were sleeping and woke up because an alarm came?
tone: Informal, conversational, asking a fan a question.
--- word ---
term: 자나가
type: Verb
breakdown: 자다 + -다가
meaning: while sleeping
notes: This is likely a transcript error for 자다가 (ja-da-ga). The grammar pattern V-다가 indicates that an action was interrupted by another. For example, '공부하다가 잠들었어요' (I fell asleep while studying).
--- word ---
term: 깼는데
type: Verb
breakdown: 깨다 + -었- + -는데
meaning: woke up, and/but
notes: The -는데 ending connects clauses to provide background context, show contrast, or introduce a topic. It's very common in spoken Korean and is softer than a simple 'and' or 'but'. Here, it sets up the situation for the following question.
--- word ---
term: 알람이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 알람 + -이
meaning: an alarm
notes: 알람 (allam) is a loanword from English 'alarm'. The particle -이 marks it as the subject of the verb. -이 is used after nouns ending in a consonant, while -가 is used after nouns ending in a vowel.
--- word ---
term: 왔어?
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + -았- + -어?
meaning: did it come?
notes: This is an informal past-tense question using 반말 (banmal) speech. While 오다 means 'to come', Koreans often say '알람이 오다' (an alarm comes) or '알람이 울리다' (an alarm rings) to mean an alarm goes off.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-다가
meaning: Indicates an action is interrupted by another action. 'While doing V...'
example: 길을 걷다가 친구를 만났어요. (I met a friend while walking down the street.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-는데
meaning: A connective ending that provides background information, shows contrast, or introduces a new clause.
example: 비가 오는데, 우산 있어요? (It's raining, do you have an umbrella?)
level: Beginner

=== [1:14] 알람이 깨었네 그러면 ===
meaning: Then the alarm woke you up.
tone: Informal, conversational, making a realization.
--- word ---
term: 알람이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 알람 + -이
meaning: the alarm
notes: The subject particle -이 is attached to the noun 알람 (alarm), which ends in a consonant, to indicate that the alarm is performing the action of the verb.
--- word ---
term: 깨었네
type: Verb
breakdown: 깨우다 + -었- + -네
meaning: woke (you) up!
notes: This is from 깨우다 (to wake someone else up), not 깨다 (to wake up oneself). The ending -네 expresses mild surprise or realization. The standard contraction is 깨웠네 (kkaewonne), so 깨었네 is a slightly less common, uncontracted form.
--- word ---
term: 그러면
type: Conjunction
breakdown: 그렇다 + -(으)면
meaning: if so / then
notes: A common connector used to mean 'in that case' or 'then', based on the previous statement. In casual speech, it's often shortened to 그럼 (geureom).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-네
meaning: An informal sentence ending used to express mild surprise, realization, or admiration about something the speaker just learned or noticed.
example: 이 케이크 맛있네! (Wow, this cake is delicious!)
level: Beginner

=== [1:20] 일찍 일어나야지 ===
meaning: You should get up early.
tone: Informal, stating a general truth or mild suggestion.
--- word ---
term: 일찍
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: early
notes: A common adverb of time. Its opposite is 늦게 (neuj-ge), meaning 'late'.
--- word ---
term: 일어나야지
type: Verb
breakdown: 일어나다 + -아야지
meaning: should get up
notes: The V-아야/어야지 ending expresses intention or a sense of 'of course one should...'. It's used in informal speech (반말) and feels more like a natural consequence or a self-reminder than a strong command. For example, '이제 가야지' (I should get going now).

meaning: Alarm.

--- word ---
term: 알람
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: alarm
notes: A loanword from English ('alarm'). Using a single noun like this is common in casual conversation to refer back to the topic being discussed.

=== [1:23] 이거는 그거야 ===
meaning: This is that.
tone: Informal, explanatory. Clarifying the nature of the livestream.
--- word ---
term: 이거는
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 이거 + -는
meaning: this thing (is)
notes: 이거 (igeo) is the colloquial contraction of 이것 (igeot), meaning 'this thing'. The topic particle -는 is attached to introduce or emphasize 'this'. In speech, 이거는 is often contracted further to 이건 (igeon).
--- word ---
term: 그거야
type: Pronoun + Copula
breakdown: 그거 + -이야
meaning: is that thing
notes: 그거 (geugeo) is the colloquial contraction of 그것 (geugeot), 'that thing'. The ending -이야 is the informal 'to be' verb (이다) used after a noun ending in a vowel. The polite equivalent is -이에요.

=== [1:27] 모닝 ===
meaning: Morning.
tone: Informal, introducing a topic.
--- word ---
term: 모닝
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: morning
notes: A loanword from English ('morning'). It's often used in compound Konglish words like 모닝콜 (moning-kol, 'wake-up call') or as part of a title, which is what the speaker is doing here.

meaning: Morning.

meaning: morning
notes: The speaker repeats the word for emphasis, building up to announce the full title of her broadcast: 'Morning Live'.

=== [1:52] 일본이야 ===
meaning: I'm in Japan.
tone: Informal, declarative, answering an unstated question.
--- word ---
term: 일본이야
type: Noun + Verb ending
breakdown: 일본 + -이야
meaning: It's Japan.
notes: This is the informal, non-polite (반말) form of the verb 이다 (to be). It's used after a noun ending in a consonant. For nouns ending in a vowel, -야 is used (e.g., '학교야' - It's school). The standard polite form would be '일본이에요'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + -이야 / -야
meaning: The informal 'to be' (이다) conjugation. Used to state what something is in a casual setting with friends or people younger than you.
example: 이거 내 가방이야. (This is my bag.)
level: Beginner

=== [1:56] 일본에서 ===
meaning: From Japan.
tone: Informal, trailing off, providing location.
--- word ---
term: 일본에서
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 일본 + -에서
meaning: In/from Japan.
notes: The particle -에서 marks the location where an action takes place. It's often contrasted with -에, which marks a static location or destination. For example, '일본에 있어요' (I am in Japan) vs. '일본에서 방송하고 있어요' (I am broadcasting from Japan).

=== [2:08] 이 방 물음표가 왜 이렇게 많아 ===
meaning: Why are there so many question marks in this room (chat)?
tone: Informal, curious, slightly amused.
--- word ---
term: 이
type: Determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: This
notes: Refers to something close to the speaker. The other basic determiners are '그' (that, near the listener) and '저' (that, far from both).
--- word ---
term: 방
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Room
notes: In the context of a livestream, '방' (bang) is colloquially used to mean the 'chat room', not the physical room the speaker is in.
--- word ---
term: 물음표가
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 물음표 + -가
meaning: Question mark(s) (as subject)
notes: The particle -가 marks the noun as the subject of the sentence. It is used after nouns ending in a vowel, while -이 is used after nouns ending in a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 왜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Why
notes: The most common interrogative adverb used to ask for a reason.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + -게
meaning: Like this, so
notes: Describes the manner or extent of an action or state. It's often used to add emphasis, similar to 'so' in English. For example, '왜 이렇게 예뻐?' (Why are you so pretty?).
--- word ---
term: 많아
type: Adjective
breakdown: 많다 + -아
meaning: Are many
notes: The informal, non-polite (반말) conjugation of the adjective 많다 (to be many/much). The polite form is '많아요'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 왜 이렇게 + Adjective/Adverb
meaning: A very common colloquial pattern used to ask 'Why is it so...?' or to express surprise at the degree of something.
example: 날씨가 왜 이렇게 추워? (Why is the weather so cold?)
level: Beginner

=== [2:13] 무슨 말이냐 ===
meaning: What does that mean?
tone: Informal, questioning, slightly confused.
--- word ---
term: 무슨
type: Determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: What, what kind of
notes: Used before a noun to ask 'what kind of [noun]?'. It differs from '뭐' (what), which is a pronoun. For example, '무슨 영화 봤어?' (What kind of movie did you see?) vs. '이거 뭐야?' (What is this?).
--- word ---
term: 말이냐
type: Noun + Verb ending
breakdown: 말 + 이다 + -냐
meaning: Is it the word/saying?
notes: The ending -냐 is an interrogative (question) ending used in informal speech (반말). It can sound blunt or very direct, and is typically used between close friends. The polite equivalent would be '무슨 말이에요?'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb Stem + -냐?
meaning: An informal question ending used in casual speech (반말). It's a direct way of asking a question.
example: 지금 어디 가냐? (Where are you going now?)
level: Intermediate

=== [2:14] 잠이 덜 깼나 봐 ===
meaning: It seems you're not fully awake yet.
tone: Informal, speculative, teasing the audience.
--- word ---
term: 잠이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 잠 + -이
meaning: Sleep (as subject)
notes: The common expression for 'to wake up' is '잠이 깨다' (literally, 'sleep breaks'). Here, 잠 is the subject of the verb 깨다.
--- word ---
term: 덜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Less, not fully
notes: Indicates that an action or state is incomplete. It is the opposite of '더' (more). For example, '밥을 덜 먹었다' (I didn't finish my meal).
--- word ---
term: 깼나
type: Verb
breakdown: 깨다 + -았- + -나
meaning: Did (one) wake up?
notes: 깨다 (to wake up) in past tense. The ending -나 is a soft, informal question ending, often used when wondering about something or asking oneself a question.
--- word ---
term: 봐
type: Verb
breakdown: 보다
meaning: It seems
notes: This is part of the grammar pattern '-나 보다'. The verb 보다 (to see) loses its literal meaning and functions as part of a grammatical structure to express supposition.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -나 보다 / -(으)ㄴ가 보다
meaning: Expresses a guess or supposition based on observation ('It seems/looks like...', 'I guess...'). Use -나 보다 for verbs and 있다/없다. Use -(으)ㄴ가 보다 for adjectives.
example: 밖에 비가 오나 봐. (It seems like it's raining outside.)
level: Intermediate

=== [2:34] 물음표만 있는 거 너무 웃긴다 ===
meaning: The fact that there are only question marks is so funny.
tone: Informal, amused, observational.
--- word ---
term: 물음표만
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 물음표 + -만
meaning: Only question marks
notes: The particle -만 means 'only' or 'just'. It can be attached directly to nouns, often replacing subject or object particles.
--- word ---
term: 있는
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다 + -는
meaning: That there are
notes: This is the present tense adjectival form of 있다 (to be/exist). The ending -는 turns a verb into a modifier that can describe a noun.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Noun (bound)
breakdown: 것
meaning: The thing, the fact that
notes: 거 is the colloquial contraction of the bound noun 것 (thing, fact). It is extremely common in spoken Korean. The phrase '있는 거' nominalizes the clause 'there are only question marks'.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: So, very
notes: While traditionally meaning 'too' (implying excess), in modern colloquial Korean, it's very frequently used to mean 'very' or 'so', similar to '아주' or '정말'.
--- word ---
term: 웃긴다
type: Verb
breakdown: 웃기다 + -ㄴ다
meaning: Is funny
notes: The -ㄴ다/-는다 ending is a declarative plain form ending. In casual speech, it's used to state a fact or observation, often with a sense of realization or exclamation. The polite form would be '웃겨요'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb + -는 것
meaning: A common way to turn a verb clause into a noun phrase. It can mean 'the act of V-ing' or 'the fact that...'.
example: 음악을 듣는 것을 좋아해요. (I like listening to music.)
level: Beginner

--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -잖아(요)

=== [2:56] 호주를 넘어가는 동선이었는데 ===
meaning: ...the plan was to go over to Australia,
tone: Informal, explanatory, setting up a contrast.
--- word ---
term: 호주를
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 호주 (Australia) + -를 (object particle)
meaning: Australia (as the object)
notes: The particle -를 marks '호주' (Australia) as the object of the verb '넘어가다' (to go over to).
--- word ---
term: 넘어가는
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 넘어가다 (to go over/cross) + -는 (present tense modifier)
meaning: going over to
notes: The -는 ending turns the verb '넘어가다' into an adjective that can describe a noun. Here, it modifies '동선' (route).
--- word ---
term: 동선이었는데
type: Noun + Verb
breakdown: 동선 (route) + -이다 (to be) + -었- (past tense) + -는데 (background/contrast ending)
meaning: it was the route, but...
notes: 동선 literally means 'movement line' and is often used to refer to a planned route or itinerary. The -는데 ending provides background information ('it was the plan...') before introducing a contrasting or unexpected event.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -는데 / A + -(으)ㄴ데
meaning: A connective ending that provides background information for the following clause. It can imply a soft contrast ('...but...'), a reason ('...so...'), or simply set the scene.
example: 비가 오는데 우리 뭐 할까? (It's raining, so what should we do?)

meaning: A common way to form the passive voice, meaning 'to become N' or 'to get N-ed'. It describes a change of state.
example: 문제가 해결됐어요. (The problem was solved.)

meaning: they said it was sold out
notes: 매진됐대 is a contraction of 매진되었다고 해. The -대 ending is a very common informal way to report something you heard from someone else (e.g., a travel agent, the news). It means 'they say...' or 'I heard that...'.

=== [3:21] 그래서 여러 선택지가 있었는데 ===

breakdown:

breakdown:

=== [3:26] 이제 일본을 경유하면 ===

type: Adverb

meaning: Now

--- word ---
term: 제일
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: The most, best
notes: 제일 (jeil) is used to form the superlative. It's placed before an adjective or adverb to mean 'the most...'. It's very common in spoken Korean, often used interchangeably with 가장 (gajang).
--- word ---
term: 짧더라고
type: Adjective
breakdown: 짧다 + -더라고
meaning: it was short (I recall)

--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -더라고(요)
meaning: Expresses the speaker's recollection of a past event or state they personally observed. It adds a nuance of mild surprise or new realization.
example: 그 식당 음식이 맛있더라고. (geu sikdang eumsigi masitdeorago.) - I found that restaurant's food to be delicious.

=== [3:48] 어제 이 저녁인 건가 ===

=== [3:51] 저녁 늦게 ===

notes: Modifies the action of spending time. The phrase is '혼자 시간(을) 보내다' (to spend time alone).
--- word ---
term: 시간도
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 시간 + -도
meaning: time also
notes: 시간 means 'time'. The particle -도 means 'also' or 'too', and it replaces the object particle -을/를. Using -도 adds the nuance of 'time, among other things'.
--- word ---
term: 보낼
type: Verb
breakdown: 보내다 + -ㄹ
meaning: to spend
notes: This is the future determiner form of 보내다 (to spend time; to send). The -(으)ㄹ ending modifies a following noun or is used in certain grammar patterns to indicate a future or intended action.
--- word ---
term: 겸
type: Dependent Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: as well as
notes: Used in the pattern 'Verb + -(으)ㄹ 겸' to indicate that an action has two or more purposes. The sentence is incomplete, implying another reason was to follow.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb Stem + -(으)ㄹ 겸
meaning: Indicates that an action serves two or more purposes. It translates to 'partly to... and partly to...' or 'while also...'.
example: 운동할 겸 산책했어요. (I went for a walk, partly as exercise.)
level: Intermediate

=== [3:59] 그래 하고 왔어 ===
meaning: So, I did that and came.
tone: Informal, conversational, concluding a thought.
--- word ---
term: 그래
type: Interjection
breakdown: 그렇다 + -아/어
meaning: so / yeah
notes: A versatile conversational filler derived from 그렇다 (to be so). Here it means 'so' or 'and so...', connecting the previous thought to the action.
--- word ---
term: 하고
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 하다 + -고
meaning: and / after doing
notes: The -고 connective ending on the verb 하다 (to do). It links actions sequentially ('do A and then do B'). '그래 하고' can mean 'after deciding to do that' or 'thinking that, I...'.
--- word ---
term: 왔어
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + -았어
meaning: came
notes: The past tense, informal non-polite (반말, banmal) form of 오다 (to come). The speaker uses banmal with her fans, creating a friendly, close relationship.

=== [4:15] 남원 갔다 왔지 ===

tone: Informal, conversational, stating a fact and seeking agreement.
--- word ---
term: 남원
type: Proper Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Namwon
notes: A city in the southwest of South Korea. The speaker is referring to a recent trip.
--- word ---
term: 갔다 왔지
type: Verb Phrase
breakdown: 가다 + -았- + 오다 + -았지
meaning: went and came back
notes: A common set phrase combining 가다 (to go) and 오다 (to come) to mean a completed round trip. The -지 ending seeks confirmation or assumes the listener knows, like adding ', right?' or ', you know'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb Stem + -다(가) 오다
meaning: Expresses the action of going somewhere and then coming back, implying a completed round trip.
example: 편의점에 갔다 왔어요. (I went to the convenience store and came back.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: Sentence + -지/죠
meaning: A conversational sentence ending used to confirm information or seek agreement on a fact the listener likely knows. It softens the tone. -지 is the informal (banmal) form.
example: 오늘 춥지? (It's cold today, isn't it?)
level: Beginner

=== [4:17] 남원 갔다가 ===

meaning: after going
notes: The connective ending -다가 shows a sequence of actions. The past tense form -았다가 emphasizes that the first action (going to Namwon) was fully completed before the next action began.

pattern: Verb Stem + -다가
meaning: A connective ending that indicates a shift from one action to another. The subject of both clauses is usually the same. It can mean 'while doing A, B happened' or 'after doing A, I did B'.
example: 공부하다가 잠들었어요. (I fell asleep while studying.)

=== [4:22] 일본으로 온 거야 ===
meaning: I came to Japan.
tone: Informal, explanatory, concluding the sequence of events.
--- word ---
term: 일본으로
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 일본 + -으로
meaning: to Japan
notes: 일본 means 'Japan'. The particle -(으)로 indicates direction ('to', 'toward'). It's often interchangeable with the location particle -에 for motion verbs like 가다 (to go) and 오다 (to come).
--- word ---
term: 온
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + -ㄴ
meaning: (that) came

--- word ---
term: 거야
type: Noun + Copula
breakdown: 것 + -이야
meaning: it is that...
notes: A contraction of 것 (thing, fact) and 이야 (the informal copula 'to be'). The '-(으)ㄴ 거야' pattern is a very common way to explain a situation or state a fact with emphasis in casual conversation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄴ/는 거야
meaning: A common informal sentence ending used to explain a situation or state a fact with emphasis, like saying 'The thing is...' or 'It's that...'.
example: 어제 밤새서 지금 피곤한 거야. (The reason I'm tired now is that I stayed up all night yesterday.)

=== [4:25] 그러니까 지금 다들 너무 충격받았나 봐 ===
meaning: So it seems like everyone is really shocked right now.
tone: Informal, speculative. Guessing why her fans are quiet.
--- word ---
term: 그러니까
type: Adverb
breakdown: 그렇다 + -니까
meaning: So / Therefore
notes: A connective adverb meaning 'so' or 'for that reason'. It links a preceding reason to a following result or conclusion. It's often used at the beginning of a sentence to start an explanation or elaboration.
--- word ---
term: 지금
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Now
notes: Refers to the present moment, 'right now'. It's often contrasted with '이제' (ije), which also means 'now' but implies a change of state from the past (e.g., 'I wasn't hungry before, but 이제 I'm hungry').
--- word ---
term: 다들
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 다 + 들
meaning: Everyone
notes: A common, natural-sounding way to say 'everyone' in conversation. It combines '다' (all) with the plural marker '들'.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Too / So / Very
notes: While its original meaning is 'too (much),' implying an excess, in modern colloquial Korean, it's very frequently used to simply mean 'very' or 'really,' much like '아주' or '정말'.
--- word ---
term: 충격받았나
type: Verb
breakdown: 충격받다 + -았- + -나
meaning: was shocked?
notes: '충격받다' means 'to receive a shock'. The '-나' ending is an informal, non-polite (반말) question form used when wondering or guessing about something, often to oneself. It's part of the '-나 봐' pattern here.
--- word ---
term: 봐
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 보다
meaning: it seems / looks like
notes: This is the verb '보다' (to see) used in the common grammatical pattern '-(으)ㄴ/는/나 보다', which expresses an assumption or guess based on an observation. The speaker observes the quiet chat and guesses everyone is shocked.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -나 보다
meaning: Expresses a guess or assumption about an action or state, meaning 'it seems/looks like...'. It's used with verbs and 있다/없다. For adjectives, -(으)ㄴ가 보다 is used.
example: 밖에 비가 오나 봐요. (It seems like it's raining outside.)
level: Intermediate

=== [4:27] 채팅이 멈췄어 ===

=== [4:30] 아니 이게 이렇게 충격받을 일이야 얘들아 ===
meaning: No, is this something to be so shocked about, guys?
tone: Informal, rhetorical, playfully teasing her fans.
--- word ---
term: 아니
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: No / I mean / Wait
notes: Often used not just for 'no,' but as a conversational filler to express surprise, disagreement, or to introduce a counterpoint. Here, it's like saying, 'Wait a minute...'
--- word ---
term: 이게
type: Pronoun + particle
breakdown: 이것 + -이
meaning: This (thing)
notes: A common contraction of '이것이'. '이것' means 'this thing,' and '-이' is the subject particle. Using contractions like '이게' makes speech sound more natural and fluent.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + -게
meaning: Like this / This much
notes: An adverb formed from the descriptive verb '이렇다' (to be like this). It modifies the following verb or adjective to indicate the manner or degree.
--- word ---
term: 충격받을
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 충격받다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: (that one would be) shocked about
notes: The '-(으)ㄹ' ending changes a verb into a form that can modify a noun, indicating a future or potential action. Here, it modifies '일' (matter), creating the meaning 'a matter to be shocked about'.
--- word ---
term: 일이야
type: Noun + copula
breakdown: 일 + -이야
meaning: is a matter
notes: '일' means 'matter' or 'affair'. '-이야' is the informal, non-polite (반말) form of the verb '이다' (to be), used after a noun ending in a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 얘들아
type: Noun + vocative particle
breakdown: 얘들 + -아
meaning: Hey guys / Everyone
notes: '얘들' is a casual term for 'guys' or 'kids' (a contraction of '이 아이들'). The vocative particle '-아/야' is attached to names or pronouns to call someone in an informal (반말) context.

=== [4:37] 채팅이 멈춘 거 너무 웃기네 ===

tone: Informal, amused.
--- word ---
term: 채팅이
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 채팅 + -이
meaning: The chat (subject)
notes: '채팅' is a loanword from 'chatting'. '-이' is the subject particle used after a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 멈춘
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 멈추다 + -(으)ㄴ
meaning: that stopped
notes: The '-(으)ㄴ' ending transforms a verb into a past-tense adjective that modifies a noun. Here, '멈춘' (stopped) describes '거' (the fact).
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Noun (dependent)
breakdown: 것
meaning: the fact that / the thing that
notes: '거' is the colloquial contraction of '것' (thing, fact). It's used here to nominalize the entire preceding clause '채팅이 멈춘' (the chat stopped), turning it into the subject of the sentence.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: So / Very
notes: A common adverb used to intensify an adjective or another adverb. In casual speech, it's often used interchangeably with '정말' (really).
--- word ---
term: 웃기네
type: Verb
breakdown: 웃기다 + -네
meaning: is funny!
notes: '웃기다' means 'to be funny'. The sentence ending '-네' expresses the speaker's personal observation, realization, or mild surprise upon noticing something. It adds a nuance of 'Oh, I see that...'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)ㄴ 것
meaning: A way to turn a verb clause into a noun phrase, meaning 'the act of V-ing' or 'the fact that...'. It allows a whole action to function as a subject or object in a larger sentence.
example: 매일 운동하는 것은 어려워요. (Exercising every day is difficult.)

=== [4:54] 또 10시간 넘게 비행을 해야 되잖아 ===
meaning: I have to fly for over 10 hours again, you know.
tone: Informal, explanatory. Stating a known fact to her audience.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Again
notes: Indicates the repetition of an action. She's referring to her upcoming flight to Australia.
--- word ---
term: 10시간
type: Noun
breakdown: 열 + 시간
meaning: 10 hours
notes: A combination of the native Korean number '열' (ten) and the counter '시간' (hour). Native Korean numbers are used for counting hours, age, and general objects.
--- word ---
term: 넘게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 넘다 + -게
meaning: Over / More than
notes: Formed by adding the adverbial suffix '-게' to the verb '넘다' (to exceed, to cross). It modifies the verb phrase to mean 'for more than 10 hours'.
--- word ---
term: 비행을
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 비행 + -을
meaning: Flight (object)
notes: '비행' (飛行) means 'flight'. The construction '[Noun]-을/를 하다' is a common way to form a verb phrase, meaning 'to do [Noun]'. So, '비행을 하다' means 'to fly'.
--- word ---
term: 해야
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + -아야
meaning: have to do
notes: Part of the '-(아/어)야 하다/되다' grammar pattern, which expresses obligation or necessity ('must', 'have to'). '해야' is a contraction of '하여야'.
--- word ---
term: 되잖아
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 되다 + -잖아
meaning: you know
notes: '되다' completes the '-(아/어)야 되다' pattern. The ending '-잖아(요)' is used to state a fact that the speaker assumes the listener also knows, similar to saying '..., right?' or 'as you know...'. It creates a sense of shared understanding.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(아/어)야 되다/하다
meaning: Expresses obligation or necessity, equivalent to 'must' or 'have to' in English.
example: 내일 일찍 일어나야 돼요. (I have to wake up early tomorrow.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -잖아(요)
meaning: A sentence ending used to confirm information the speaker believes the listener already knows. It can be translated as '..., you know' or 'as you know...'.
example: 어제 우리 만났잖아. (We met yesterday, you know.)
level: Intermediate

=== [4:59] 근데 여기 너무 늦게 도착을 했잖아 ===
meaning: But I arrived here so late, you know.
tone: Informal, explanatory. Stating a fact the listener might already know or could guess.
--- word ---
term: 근데
type: Adverb
breakdown: 그런데 (contraction)
meaning: But, by the way
notes: A very common contraction of 그런데 (geureonde). It's used in casual conversation to mean 'but' or to change the subject, similar to 'by the way'.
--- word ---
term: 여기
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: Here
notes: Refers to her hotel in Japan. It's a fundamental location word, along with 거기 (geogi - there) and 저기 (jeogi - over there).
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Too, so, very
notes: An intensifier. While it literally means 'too much' (implying excess), in colloquial speech, it's often used interchangeably with 아주 (aju) or 정말 (jeongmal) to simply mean 'very' or 'really'.
--- word ---
term: 늦게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 늦다 + -게
meaning: Late
notes: The -게 (-ge) ending is attached to an adjective stem (늦다 - to be late) to turn it into an adverb, describing how an action is performed. For example, '빠르게' (ppareuge - quickly) from 빠르다 (ppareuda - to be fast).
--- word ---
term: 도착을
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 도착 + -을
meaning: Arrival (as object)
notes: 도착 (dochak) is 'arrival'. It's often paired with the verb 하다 (hada) to mean 'to arrive' (도착하다). Here, the object particle -을 (-eul) is used, making 'arrival' the object of the verb 'did' (했다 - haetda). This is a common structure: Noun + 하다 can often be split into Noun-을/를 하다.
--- word ---
term: 했잖아
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + -았- + -잖아
meaning: I did, you know
notes: The -잖아 (-janha) ending is used to state a fact that the speaker assumes the listener already knows or should agree with. It creates a sense of shared context, like saying '...you know?' or '...as you know.' It's very common in informal, spoken Korean.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-잖아(요)
meaning: Used to confirm or remind the listener of something they both know. It can sound like '...you know,' '...of course,' or 'Come on, ...'.
example: 어제 말했잖아. (Eoje malhaetjana.) - I told you yesterday, you know.
level: Beginner

=== [5:03] 그래서 잠은 안 오고 ===
meaning: So I couldn't sleep, and...
tone: Informal, trailing off. Explaining a sequence of events.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: So, therefore
notes: A standard connector used to show a cause-and-effect relationship. It links the previous sentence (arriving late) to the current one (not being able to sleep).
--- word ---
term: 잠은
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 잠 + -은
meaning: Sleep (as for)
notes: 잠 (jam) means 'sleep'. The topic particle -은/는 (-eun/neun) is used here to add a nuance of contrast. It implies 'As for sleep (unlike other things), it didn't come'.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Not
notes: This is the most common way to negate verbs and adjectives in Korean. It is placed directly before the verb/adjective. The alternative is the V/A-지 않다 (ji anta) construction, e.g., 오지 않다 (oji anta).
--- word ---
term: 오고
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + -고
meaning: come and...
notes: The expression for 'to feel sleepy' is 잠이 오다 (jami oda), literally 'sleep comes'. The -고 (-go) ending is a connector that means 'and' or 'and then', linking this clause to the next action. The sentence is left unfinished, a common feature of spoken language.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고
meaning: A connective ending that links two clauses. It can indicate a simple sequence of actions ('and' or 'and then') or list two or more actions or states.
example: 밥을 먹고 영화를 봤어요. (Babeul meokgo yeonghwareul bwasseoyo.) - I ate and then watched a movie.
level: Beginner

=== [5:06] 이래서 한 번 좀 조금이라도 바깥 구경 좀 할겸 ===
meaning: So for that reason, to also get a little bit of a look outside...
tone: Informal, explanatory, incomplete sentence. Setting up the reason for her next action.
--- word ---
term: 이래서
type: Adverbial phrase
breakdown: 이렇다 + -아서
meaning: For this reason, so
notes: A colloquial contraction of 이리하여서 (irihayeoseo). It means 'because of this' or 'this is why', referring to the previously mentioned reasons (arriving late, can't sleep).
--- word ---
term: 한 번
type: Noun phrase
breakdown: 
meaning: Once, one time
notes: Literally 'one time'. It can also mean 'to give something a try'. For example, 한번 해봐 (hanbeon haebwa) means 'try it once'.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금 (contraction)
meaning: A little, please
notes: A contraction of 조금 (jogeum). It's frequently used in spoken Korean to soften a statement or request, making it sound more polite or casual, rather than demanding.
--- word ---
term: 조금이라도
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 조금 + -이라도
meaning: Even a little bit
notes: The particle -(이)라도 (-irado) means 'even' or 'at least', often suggesting that the chosen option is not the best but is better than nothing. Here, it implies 'even if it's just for a little while'.
--- word ---
term: 바깥
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: The outside
notes: Refers to the area outside a building or room. It's similar to 밖 (bak), but 바깥 (bakkat) can have a slightly broader or more emphatic feel.
--- word ---
term: 구경
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Sightseeing, looking around
notes: Paired with 하다 (hada), 구경하다 (gugyeonghada) means 'to look around' or 'to go sightseeing'. Here it's used with 할겸 (halgyeom).
--- word ---
term: 할겸
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 하다 + -(으)ㄹ겸
meaning: for the purpose of doing...
notes: The -(으)ㄹ겸 (-eulgyeom) grammar pattern indicates that an action is done for two or more purposes. Here, the first purpose is 'to look outside', and the second purpose is implied or stated in the next sentence.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄹ겸
meaning: Indicates that an action has two or more purposes. It's often used as A-(으)ㄹ겸 B-(으)ㄹ겸 to mean 'for the purpose of doing A and also doing B'.
example: 운동할겸 산책했어요. (Undonghalgyeom sanchaekhaesseoyo.) - I took a walk to get some exercise.
level: Intermediate

=== [5:13] 돈키호텔에 갔다 왔어 ===
meaning: I went to Donki-hotel.
tone: Informal, declarative. Note the speaker's cute mistake.
--- word ---
term: 돈키호텔에
type: Proper Noun + Particle
breakdown: 돈키호텔 + -에
meaning: To Donki-hotel
notes: The speaker misspeaks the name of the famous Japanese store 'Don Quijote' (돈키호테) as 'Donki-hotel' (돈키호텔). This is a personal quirk mentioned in the story bible. The particle -에 (-e) indicates a destination or location.
--- word ---
term: 갔다 왔어
type: Verb phrase
breakdown: 가다 + -았- + 오다 + -았어
meaning: Went and came back
notes: This is a very common compound verb construction. It combines 가다 (gada - to go) and 오다 (oda - to come) to mean that the speaker went somewhere and has since returned. It's the standard way to say 'I went to...' when you are no longer at that place.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 왔다
meaning: Indicates that the subject went somewhere, did something, and then came back. It emphasizes the completion of a round trip.
example: 친구 만나고 왔어. (Chingu mannago wasseo.) - I met my friend (and came back).
level: Beginner

=== [5:17] 돈키호테에서 조용히 혼자서 좀 구경이나 해볼까 하면서 이렇게 왔다 ===
meaning: I went there thinking, 'Maybe I'll just quietly look around by myself at Don Quijote.'
tone: Informal, narrative. Explaining her thought process leading to an action.
--- word ---
term: 돈키호테에서
type: Proper Noun + Particle
breakdown: 돈키호테 + -에서
meaning: At Don Quijote
notes: She says the correct name here. The particle -에서 (-eseo) marks the location where an action takes place, different from -에 (-e) which marks a static location or destination.
--- word ---
term: 조용히
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조용하다 + -히
meaning: Quietly
notes: An adverb formed by adding -히 (-hi) to the adjective stem 조용하다 (joyonghada - to be quiet). This is one of the common ways to form adverbs in Korean.
--- word ---
term: 혼자서
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 혼자 + -서
meaning: Alone, by myself
notes: 혼자 (honja) means 'alone'. The particle -서 (-seo) is often attached for emphasis, but 혼자 can also be used by itself with the same meaning.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금 (contraction)
meaning: A little, just
notes: Here, it softens the intention, making it sound more casual and less definite, like 'I'll just have a look around'.
--- word ---
term: 구경이나
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 구경 + -이나
meaning: Sightseeing or something
notes: The particle -(이)나 (-ina) attached to a noun suggests it's not the primary or best choice, but it's a decent alternative. It adds a nuance of 'just' or 'or something', indicating a casual decision, like 'I guess I'll just look around'.
--- word ---
term: 해볼까
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + -아/어 보다 + -(으)ㄹ까
meaning: Shall I try doing...
notes: This combines two patterns: -아/어 보다 (-a/eo boda), which means 'to try doing something', and -(으)ㄹ까 (-eulkka), which is used to ask oneself a question or make a suggestion. Together, it means 'I wonder if I should try...'
--- word ---
term: 하면서
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + -(으)면서
meaning: While thinking/saying
notes: The -(으)면서 (-eumyeonseo) pattern means 'while doing'. When used with 하다 (hada) after a quote or thought, it means 'while thinking/saying that...'. It connects her thought ('해볼까') to her action ('왔다').
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Like this
notes: Refers back to the manner or reason just described. It connects the thought process to the final action.
--- word ---
term: 왔다
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + -았/었-
meaning: Came
notes: Here, it means 'I came here' or 'I ended up here', referring to her being on the livestream after her trip to the store.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Quoted thought + -(으)면서
meaning: Used to express that an action was performed while having a certain thought. The thought is often in the -(으)ㄹ까 하다 (thinking of doing) form.
example: 커피나 마실까 하면서 카페에 갔다. (Keopina masilkka hamyeonseo kape-e gatda.) - I went to the cafe, thinking 'maybe I'll just have some coffee'.
level: Intermediate

=== [5:27] 1번...1분도 진짜 워낙 조용해가지고... ===
meaning: Japan... it was just so quiet, so...
tone: Informal, trailing off, slightly flustered. She seems to misspeak.
--- word ---
term: 1번...1분도
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Number 1... even 1 minute
notes: This is likely a slip of the tongue for 일본 (ilbon - Japan). The speaker says 'il-beon' (number 1) and 'il-bun' (1 minute) instead. This kind of mistake is common in fast, casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Really, truly
notes: A very common adverb used in spoken Korean to add emphasis. It's similar to 정말 (jeongmal) but can feel slightly more casual.
--- word ---
term: 워낙
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: So, very, by nature
notes: This adverb emphasizes that a certain quality is inherent or extreme. It carries the nuance of 'naturally so' or 'exceptionally'. Here, it means Japan was exceptionally quiet.
--- word ---
term: 조용해가지고...
type: Verb
breakdown: 조용하다 + -아/어지다 + -아/어 가지고
meaning: it became quiet and so...
notes: A combination of two grammar points. -아/어지다 (-a/eojida) changes an adjective into a verb meaning 'to become [adjective]'. -아/어 가지고 (-a/eo gajigo) is a colloquial connector meaning 'because' or 'and then', similar to -아/어서 (-a/eoseo). The sentence trails off.

=== [5:28] 오랜만인거야 생각해보니까 ===
meaning: It had been a while, now that I think about it.
tone: Informal, reflective. Sharing a realization with the audience.
--- word ---
term: 오랜만인거야
type: Noun phrase + Copula
breakdown: 오랜만 + -인 + 것 + -이야
meaning: It's been a long time
notes: This is the colloquial -는/은/인 거야 (-neun/eun/in geoya) sentence ending. It's used to explain or state a fact with a sense of realization or emphasis. 오랜만 (oraenman) means 'a long time'. The full form is 오랜만인 것이야 (oraenmanin geos-iya).
--- word ---
term: 생각해보니까
type: Verb
breakdown: 생각하다 + -아/어 보다 + -(으)니까
meaning: Now that I think about it
notes: A very common phrase. The -(으)니까 (-eunikka) ending here indicates a discovery or realization that occurs as a result of the action in the first clause. It means 'I did X, and as a result, I realized Y'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)니까
meaning: A connective ending that can mean 'because', but in this context, it indicates a discovery made after performing an action. It translates to 'When I did X, I found that Y' or 'Now that I do X...'.
example: 집에 와보니까 아무도 없었다. (Jibe wabonikka amudo eopseotda.) - When I came home, I found that nobody was there.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: ~는/은/인 거야
meaning: A colloquial sentence ending used to explain a situation or emphasize a fact, as if revealing a reason. It's the informal version of ~는/은/인 것이다.
example: 그래서 화가 난 거야. (Geuraeseo hwaga nan geoya.) - That's the reason I got angry.
level: Intermediate

=== [5:32] 근데 너무 많이 사버린 거야 ===
meaning: But I ended up buying way too much.
tone: Informal, emphatic, slightly regretful. Explaining an unfortunate outcome.
--- word ---
term: 근데
type: Adverb
breakdown: 그런데 (contraction)
meaning: But
notes: A casual contraction of 그런데 (geureonde), used to introduce a contrasting idea. She went just to look around, BUT she bought too much.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Too, so, very
notes: An intensifier. Here it clearly means 'too much', indicating an excessive amount that has become a problem.
--- word ---
term: 많이
type: Adverb
breakdown: 많다 + -이
meaning: A lot, much
notes: The adverbial form of the adjective 많다 (manta - to be many/much). It modifies the verb 'to buy' (사다).
--- word ---
term: 사버린
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 사다 + -아/어 버리다 + -ㄴ/은
meaning: (that I) ended up buying
notes: The verb 사다 (sada - to buy) is combined with the auxiliary verb -아/어 버리다 (-a/eo beorida), which implies the action was completed, often with a sense of regret, relief, or surprise. The -ㄴ (-n) ending turns the verb into an adjective modifying the noun 거야 (geoya).
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: Noun + Copula
breakdown: 것 + -이야
meaning: it is the thing that...
notes: The colloquial -는/은/인 거야 (-neun/eun/in geoya) ending again. It emphasizes the statement, presenting it as an explanation for her current situation (e.g., having too much stuff).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 버리다
meaning: An auxiliary verb that indicates the complete, and often irreversible, finishing of an action. It can imply regret (as in this case), relief, or that the action was done without forethought.
example: 케이크를 다 먹어 버렸어. (Keikeureul da meogeo beoryeosseo.) - I went and ate all the cake (and now it's gone).
level: Intermediate

=== [5:38] 그래서 지금 큰일 났어 ===
meaning: So now I'm in big trouble.
tone: Informal, dramatic, expressing worry.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: So, therefore
notes: A common conjunction that connects a reason/cause to a result. It's used to show a logical consequence.
--- word ---
term: 지금
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Now
notes: Refers to the present moment. Often contrasted with 이제 (ije), which also means 'now' but implies a change from a previous state (e.g., 'from now on').
--- word ---
term: 큰일
type: Noun
breakdown: 크다 + 일
meaning: A big problem, a serious matter
notes: A compound noun from 크다 (to be big) and 일 (matter, affair, problem). The phrase '큰일 났어' (keunil nasseo) is a very common idiom meaning 'I'm in big trouble' or 'Something terrible has happened'.
--- word ---
term: 났어
type: Verb
breakdown: 나다 + -았어
meaning: happened, occurred
notes: This is the past tense, informal non-polite (반말, banmal) form of the verb 나다 (to occur, to break out). It's used when speaking to friends or people of the same age or younger.

=== [5:41] 호주 갈 때 캐리어 ===
meaning: The suitcase for when I go to Australia...
tone: Informal, trailing off, setting up a problem.
--- word ---
term: 호주
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Australia
notes: The Korean name for Australia.
--- word ---
term: 갈
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 가다 + -ㄹ
meaning: that will go
notes: This is the future adnominal (or modifier) form of 가다 (to go). It modifies the following noun, 때 (time), creating the phrase 'the time of going'.
--- word ---
term: 때
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: When, at the time of
notes: A bound noun that cannot be used alone. It must be preceded by a modifier, typically a verb, to specify a point in time (e.g., 먹을 때 - when eating).
--- word ---
term: 캐리어
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Suitcase
notes: This is a Konglish (Korean-style English) word, derived from 'carrier'. The sentence trails off here, leaving the listener to infer the problem with the suitcase.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄹ 때
meaning: Indicates the time 'when' an action takes place. It attaches to a verb stem.
example: 비가 올 때 집에 있었어요. (I was at home when it rained.)
level: Beginner

=== [5:44] 캐리어를 어떻게 써야 될지 모르겠다 ===
meaning: I don't know how I should use the suitcase.
tone: Informal, expressing confusion and worry.
--- word ---
term: 캐리어를
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 캐리어 + -를
meaning: the suitcase (as an object)
notes: The particle -를 marks '캐리어' (suitcase) as the direct object of the verb '써야 될지' (should use).
--- word ---
term: 어떻게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 어떻다 + -게
meaning: How
notes: A question word used to ask about the manner or method of an action. Learners often confuse it with the expression 어떡해 (what should I do?).
--- word ---
term: 써야 될지
type: Verb phrase
breakdown: 쓰다 + -어야 되다 + -ㄹ지
meaning: how I should use
notes: A combination of two grammar patterns. 쓰다 here means 'to use'. -어야 되다 means 'must/should', and -(으)ㄹ지 is used with question words to express uncertainty.
--- word ---
term: 모르겠다
type: Verb
breakdown: 모르다 + -겠- + -다
meaning: I don't know
notes: The -겠- suffix here expresses the speaker's current state of mind or conjecture ('I guess I don't know'). The -다 ending is a plain form often used for self-talk or narration.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Question word + V-(으)ㄹ지 모르다
meaning: Used to express uncertainty about how, what, when, where, etc., to do something.
example: 점심에 뭘 먹을지 모르겠어요. (I don't know what to eat for lunch.)
level: Intermediate

=== [5:48] 맞아 이거 들고 호주 가야 돼 ===
meaning: That's right, I have to take this with me to Australia.
tone: Informal, agreeing with a fan, resigned.
--- word ---
term: 맞아
type: Interjection/Verb
breakdown: 맞다 + -아
meaning: That's right
notes: A very common informal (반말, banmal) expression used to agree with what someone has said. The base verb 맞다 means 'to be correct'.
--- word ---
term: 이거
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 이것 (contraction)
meaning: This thing, this
notes: A colloquial contraction of 이것. It's extremely common in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 들고
type: Verb
breakdown: 들다 + -고
meaning: holding and...
notes: The verb 들다 means 'to hold' or 'to carry'. The -고 ending connects two actions: first you hold it, and then you do the next action (go to Australia).
--- word ---
term: 호주
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Australia
notes: The Korean name for Australia.
--- word ---
term: 가야 돼
type: Verb phrase
breakdown: 가다 + -아야 되다
meaning: have to go
notes: This is the informal (반말, banmal) form of the grammar pattern V-아야/어야 하다/되다, which expresses obligation or necessity. 돼 is a contraction of 되어.

=== [5:51] 어떡해 진짜 ===
meaning: What am I going to do, really?
tone: Informal, exasperated, rhetorical.
--- word ---
term: 어떡해
type: Expression
breakdown: 어떻게 해 (contraction)
meaning: What should I do?
notes: A very common phrase to express worry, frustration, or being at a loss. It's a contraction of '어떻게 해?' (How should I handle this?). Don't confuse it with the adverb '어떻게' (how).
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Really, seriously
notes: Used frequently in spoken Korean to add emphasis, similar to 'for real' or 'truly' in English.

=== [5:54] 동키호텔을 가는 게 아니었어 ===
meaning: I shouldn't have gone to Don Quijote.
tone: Informal, regretful, humorous.
--- word ---
term: 동키호텔을
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 동키호텔 + -을
meaning: Don Quijote (as an object)
notes: The speaker humorously mispronounces the store name '돈키호테' (Don Quijote) as '동키호텔' (Donki-hotel). The -을 is the object particle.
--- word ---
term: 가는
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 가다 + -는
meaning: going
notes: The -는 particle turns the verb 가다 (to go) into a noun phrase when combined with 것 (or its contraction 게), meaning 'the act of going'.
--- word ---
term: 게
type: Noun (contraction)
breakdown: 것 + 이
meaning: the thing that is...
notes: 게 is the common spoken contraction of the noun phrase '것이' (the thing is).
--- word ---
term: 아니었어
type: Verb
breakdown: 아니다 + -었어
meaning: was not
notes: The past tense, informal (반말, banmal) form of 아니다 (to not be). The full pattern expresses regret for a past action.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 게 아니었다
meaning: Expresses regret for a past action, meaning 'I shouldn't have done V'.
example: 그 말을 하는 게 아니었어. (I shouldn't have said that.)
level: Intermediate

=== [5:56] 이것도 누르고 누른 거야 ===

tone: Informal, defensive, emphatic.
--- word ---
term: 이것도
type: Pronoun + particle
breakdown: 이것 + -도
meaning: This too, even this
notes: The particle -도 means 'also' or 'even'. The speaker is emphasizing that even this large amount is the result of self-restraint.
--- word ---
term: 누르고
type: Verb
breakdown: 누르다 + -고
meaning: suppressing and...
notes: The verb 누르다 literally means 'to press down'. Here, it's used idiomatically to mean 'to suppress' or 'hold back' an urge, like the desire to shop more.
--- word ---
term: 누른
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 누르다 + -ㄴ
meaning: (that I) suppressed
notes: The past adnominal form of 누르다. The repetition of '누르고 누른' emphasizes the action of holding back multiple times.
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: Noun + copula (contraction)
breakdown: 것 + 이야
meaning: it is the thing that...
notes: A common informal sentence ending (-(으)ㄴ/는 거야) used to state a fact with emphasis or provide an explanation, as if revealing a piece of information.

=== [5:58] 롯봉기점으로 갔거든 ===
meaning: You see, I went to the Roppongi branch.
tone: Informal, explanatory, giving a reason.
--- word ---
term: 롯폰기점으로
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 롯폰기점 + -으로
meaning: to the Roppongi branch
notes: 롯폰기 (Roppongi) is a district in Tokyo. 점 (jeom) means 'branch' or 'store'. The particle -(으)로 indicates direction.
--- word ---
term: 갔거든
type: Verb
breakdown: 가다 + -았- + -거든
meaning: I went, you see
notes: The sentence ending -거든 is used in conversation to provide a reason or background information for a previous statement. It has a nuance of 'let me explain...' or 'because...'. It is primarily used in spoken Korean.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-거든(요)
meaning: A sentence ending that provides a reason or background information for what was just said. It implies the listener might not be aware of this fact.
example: A: 왜 늦었어요? (Why are you late?) B: 길이 많이 막혔거든. (Because the traffic was really bad, you see.)
level: Intermediate

=== [6:03] 1층부터 6층까지 있더라 ===
meaning: It had (I saw that it had) floors from the 1st to the 6th.
tone: Informal, observational, recalling a past discovery.
--- word ---
term: 1층부터
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 1층 (il-cheung) + -부터
meaning: from the 1st floor
notes: The particle -부터 means 'from' or 'starting from' a certain time or place. It is often paired with -까지 ('to, until').
--- word ---
term: 6층까지
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 6층 (yuk-cheung) + -까지
meaning: to the 6th floor
notes: The particle -까지 means 'to', 'until', or 'up to' a certain time or place. The pattern 'A부터 B까지' ('from A to B') is very common.
--- word ---
term: 있더라
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다 + -더라
meaning: there was (I recall seeing)
notes: -더라 is a retrospective verb ending used in informal speech. It indicates that the speaker is recalling something they personally saw, heard, or experienced in the past, often with a sense of mild surprise or new realization. For example, '가 보니까 정말 예쁘더라' ('When I went, I saw it was really pretty').
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + 부터 + Noun + 까지
meaning: Indicates a range from a starting point to an ending point, meaning 'from A to B'.
example: 월요일부터 금요일까지 일해요. (I work from Monday to Friday.)
level: Beginner

=== [6:05] 5층인가까지 있잖아 ===
meaning: It goes up to like, the 5th floor, you know?
tone: Informal, self-correcting, seeking agreement.
--- word ---
term: 5층인가
type: Noun + Ending
breakdown: 5층 (o-cheung) + -인가
meaning: the 5th floor, or something?
notes: -(으)ㄴ가/인가 is attached to nouns and verbs to express uncertainty, speculation, or a guess. It's like saying 'I think...' or 'maybe...'. Here, she is correcting her previous statement and is not entirely sure.
--- word ---
term: 까지
type: Particle
breakdown: 
meaning: up to
notes: The particle -까지 means 'to', 'until', or 'up to' a certain time or place. Here it marks the endpoint of the store's floors.
--- word ---
term: 있잖아
type: Verb Ending
breakdown: 있다 + -잖아
meaning: there is, you know
notes: -잖아 is a very common informal ending used to state a fact the speaker assumes the listener knows or should agree with. It creates a friendly, inclusive tone, like saying '...you know?' or '...right?'. Overuse can sometimes sound insistent.

=== [6:09] 일단 1층에서 장바구니 반절이 좀 찬 거야 ===
meaning: So first, on the 1st floor, my shopping basket was already about half full.
tone: Informal, narrative, setting the scene.
--- word ---
term: 일단
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: first of all, for now
notes: 일단 (ildan) is used when prioritizing an action or stating what will be done first before other things. It's similar to 'for the time being' or 'first things first'.
--- word ---
term: 1층에서
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 1층 (il-cheung) + -에서
meaning: on the 1st floor
notes: The particle -에서 marks the location where an action takes place. It's different from -에, which typically marks a static location, destination, or time.
--- word ---
term: 장바구니
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: shopping basket
notes: A compound word from 장 (jang, 'market') and 바구니 (baguni, 'basket').
--- word ---
term: 반절이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 반절 (ban-jeol) + -이
meaning: half (as the subject)
notes: 반절 means 'half'. It's similar to just 반 (ban). The particle -이 marks it as the subject of the verb 'to be full' (차다).
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a bit, somewhat
notes: 좀 is a contraction of 조금 (jogeum). In spoken Korean, it's used very frequently to soften requests or statements, making them sound less direct or abrupt.
--- word ---
term: 찬
type: Verb (Adnominal form)
breakdown: 차다 + -ㄴ
meaning: full
notes: This is the past adnominal form of 차다 (chada, 'to be full'). It modifies the following noun, '거' (geo, 'thing/fact').
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: Noun + Ending
breakdown: 것 + -이야
meaning: it is (the fact that)
notes: This is a contraction of '것이야' (geosiya). The '-(으)ㄴ/는 거야' pattern is a very common way to end sentences in informal, narrative speech. It states a fact or explains a situation with a sense of finality or emphasis.

=== [6:12] 그래서 어? 1층만 보고 가야 되나? 싶은 거야 ===
meaning: So I was like, 'Huh? Should I just see the 1st floor and go?'
tone: Informal, quoting an internal thought, expressing hesitation.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, therefore
notes: A standard conjunction used to show a cause-and-effect relationship. 'A. 그래서 B.' means 'A happened, so B happened.'
--- word ---
term: 어?
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Huh? What?
notes: A common sound to express surprise, confusion, or to get someone's attention.
--- word ---
term: 1층만
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 1층 (il-cheung) + -만
meaning: only the 1st floor
notes: The particle -만 means 'only' or 'just'. It singles out the noun it's attached to, excluding other possibilities.
--- word ---
term: 보고
type: Verb
breakdown: 보다 + -고
meaning: see and...
notes: The -고 ending connects two clauses or actions in sequence. 'A고 B' means 'do A and then do B'.
--- word ---
term: 가야 되나?
type: Verb Phrase
breakdown: 가다 + -아야 되다 + -나?
meaning: should I go?
notes: A combination of -아야/어야 되다 ('must/have to') and the self-questioning ending -나?. It expresses the speaker wondering to herself about an obligation or necessity.
--- word ---
term: 싶은 거야
type: Verb Phrase
breakdown: 싶다 + -은 + 것 + -이야
meaning: I was thinking/feeling
notes: The pattern '...싶은 거야' is used to describe a thought or feeling one had. 싶다 (sipda) here means 'to think' or 'to feel like'. The full phrase literally means 'It was the feeling that...'
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb-아야/어야 되다/하다
meaning: Expresses obligation or necessity, equivalent to 'must' or 'have to'.
example: 지금 숙제를 해야 돼요. (I have to do my homework now.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: Direct Quote + 싶은 생각이 들다 / 싶은 거야
meaning: Used to express one's inner thoughts or feelings, like saying 'I had the thought that...' or 'I was feeling like...'
example: '이거 살까?' 싶은 생각이 들었어요. (I had the thought, 'Should I buy this?')
level: Intermediate

=== [6:20] 그니까 동키호텔은 그렇잖아 ===

=== [6:31] 뭐 한 4층까지만 일단 구경이라도 해보자 하면서 ===

meaning: about, approximately
notes: When placed before a number or quantity, 한 (han) means 'about' or 'around'. Do not confuse it with 한 (han) meaning 'one'.
--- word ---

--- word ---

--- word ---

--- word ---
term: 해보자

breakdown: 해보다 + -자
meaning: let's try to do
notes: 해보다 (haeboda) is a compound verb from 하다 ('to do') and 보다 ('to see'), meaning 'to try doing'. The -자 ending is an informal proposal, like 'let's...'.
--- word ---
term: 하면서
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + -면서
meaning: while thinking/saying
notes: When used after a quote or thought, '...하면서' means 'while saying...' or 'while thinking...'. It connects the thought process to the subsequent action.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + (이)라도
meaning: Indicates choosing a less-preferred but still acceptable option. Means 'at least' or 'even just'.
example: 커피가 없으면 물이라도 주세요. (If there's no coffee, please give me at least water.)

=== [6:42] 지하에서도 살짝 찬 거야 ===

breakdown: 
meaning: slightly, a little bit

meaning: it is the thing that...

pattern: -(으)ㄴ 거야
meaning: Used to explain a past event or situation, often with a slightly emphatic or narrative tone. It's like saying 'the thing is, [this] happened.'

=== [6:45] 돌아가야 되나 호텔로? ===
meaning: Should I go back to the hotel?
tone: Informal, self-questioning, rhetorical.
--- word ---
term: 돌아가야
type: Verb
breakdown: 돌아가다 (to go back) + -아야 하다 (must do)
meaning: have to go back
notes: The grammar pattern '-아야/어야 하다' (or 되다) means 'must' or 'have to'. Here, it's combined with the question ending '-나' to express deliberation.
--- word ---
term: 되나
type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 되다 (to be okay) + -나? (question ending)
meaning: I wonder if I should...
notes: The ending '-나?' is an informal question form used when asking oneself a question or expressing doubt/wonder. It's softer and more speculative than a direct question ending like '-니?' or '-어?'.

--- grammar ---
pattern: -아야/어야 되나(요)?
meaning: Expresses wondering or deliberating about an obligation or necessity. It translates to 'Should I...?' or 'I wonder if I have to...'.
example: 지금 출발해야 되나? (Should I leave now?)

=== [6:50] 이러다가 궁금하잖아 ===
meaning: But doing that, I got curious, you know?
tone: Informal, explanatory, seeking agreement from the audience.
--- word ---
term: 이러다가
type: Verb
breakdown: 이렇다 (to be like this) + -다가 (while doing)
meaning: while doing this / at this rate
notes: '-다가' is a connective ending indicating that the action in the first clause is interrupted or followed by a different action in the second clause. '이러다가' means 'while I was doing/thinking this...' or 'at this rate...'
--- word ---

meaning: it's curious, you know
notes: The ending '-잖아(요)' is used to state something the speaker assumes the listener already knows or would agree with. It creates a sense of shared understanding and can be translated as '...you know', '...of course', or '...isn't it?'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -잖아(요)
meaning: A sentence ending used to confirm a fact or seek agreement on something the speaker believes is obvious or already known to the listener.
example: 날씨가 춥잖아. (The weather's cold, you know.)
level: Beginner

=== [6:52] 2층을 갔어 ===

--- word ---
term: 2층을
type: Noun + Particle

meaning: the 2nd floor
notes: While the location particle '-에' is standard with '가다' (to go), using the object particle '-을/를' is common in colloquial speech. It can add a nuance of traversing or 'doing' the location, like 'I did the 2nd floor'.

=== [7:01] 더 찼더라고 ===
meaning: It got even more full, I realized.
tone: Informal, retrospective, sharing a past observation.
--- word ---
term: 더

breakdown: 
meaning: more
notes: A simple adverb used to indicate a greater degree or quantity. It precedes the verb or adjective it modifies.
--- word ---

=== [7:06] 가득 찼더라고 ===
meaning: It was completely full, I realized.
tone: Informal, descriptive, recalling a past observation.
--- word ---
term: 가득
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: full, packed
notes: An adverb meaning 'fully' or 'to the brim'. It's often paired with the verb 차다 (chada), meaning 'to be full', to create the common phrase 가득 차다 (gadeuk chada), 'to be packed full'.
--- word ---
term: 찼더라고

meaning: it was full (I recall)

--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + ~더라고(요)
meaning: Expresses the speaker's recollection of a past event or state they personally observed. It often carries a nuance of a new realization or mild surprise.

=== [7:11] 그래서 거의 뛰쳐나오듯이 나왔어 ===
meaning: So I practically ran out of there.
tone: Informal, dramatic, exaggerating for effect.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: Conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: so, therefore
notes: A standard connector indicating a cause-and-effect relationship. It links a preceding reason to a following result.
--- word ---
term: 거의
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: almost, nearly
notes: Used to indicate that something is very close to a certain state or action but not completely so. For example, 거의 다 했어 (geoui da haesseo) means 'I'm almost finished'.
--- word ---
term: 뛰쳐나오듯이
type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 뛰쳐나오다 + ~듯이
meaning: as if running/dashing out
notes: 뛰쳐나오다 (ttwicheonaoda) is a compound verb combining 뛰다 (ttwida, to run) and 나오다 (naoda, to come out), meaning 'to dash out' or 'to storm out'. The ~듯이 (deusi) ending means 'as if' or 'like'.
--- word ---
term: 나왔어
type: Verb
breakdown: 나오다 + ~았어
meaning: I came out
notes: The informal past tense of 나오다 (naoda, to come out). The direction is towards the speaker. The opposite, 나가다 (nagada, to go out), indicates movement away from the speaker.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~듯이
meaning: Means 'as if', 'like', or 'as though'. It attaches to a verb stem to describe an action that resembles another action.
example: 그는 물을 마시듯이 술을 마신다. (He drinks alcohol as if he's drinking water.)
level: Intermediate

=== [7:16] 아니 사실 산 건 별로 없다 ===
meaning: No, actually, I didn't buy much.
tone: Informal, corrective, downplaying her purchase.
--- word ---
term: 아니
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: no, well...
notes: While it means 'no', it's often used as a conversational filler to pause, correct oneself, or soften a statement, similar to 'well, actually...' in English.
--- word ---
term: 사실
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: in fact, actually
notes: Used to introduce a true statement, often one that might be surprising or contrary to what was just said.
--- word ---
term: 산
type: Verb (Adjective Form)
breakdown: 사다 + ~(으)ㄴ
meaning: (that I) bought
notes: This is the past-tense adjective form of the verb 사다 (sada, to buy). It modifies the following noun, which in this case is 건 (geon, thing).
--- word ---
term: 건
type: Pronoun + Particle (Contracted)
breakdown: 것 + 은
meaning: the thing (as for)
notes: A common contraction of 것 (geot, 'thing' or 'the fact that') and the topic particle 은 (eun). So, 산 건 (san geon) means 'the thing that I bought'.
--- word ---
term: 별로
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not really, not much
notes: This adverb is almost always used with a negative predicate (e.g., 없다, 안, 못). A common mistake for learners is to use it in a positive sentence. For example, '별로 좋아해' is incorrect; it should be '별로 안 좋아해' (I don't really like it).
--- word ---
term: 없다
type: Adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: to not exist, to not have
notes: This is the dictionary form, or 'plain form'. Using it to end a sentence can sound like a diary entry, a statement of fact, or slightly abrupt in conversation. Here, it's used for narrative effect.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~(으)ㄴ 것
meaning: A grammar pattern that turns a past-tense verb phrase into a noun clause, meaning 'the thing that was V-ed' or 'the fact that one V-ed'.
example: 어제 먹은 것은 피자였어. (The thing I ate yesterday was pizza.)
level: Beginner

=== [7:20] 한번 보여줄게 ===

--- word ---

=== [7:21] 산 건 별로 없어 ===
meaning: I didn't buy much.

--- word ---
term: 산
type: Verb (Adjective Form)
breakdown: 사다 + ~(으)ㄴ
meaning: (that I) bought

--- word ---
term: 건
type: Pronoun + Particle (Contracted)
breakdown: 것 + 은
meaning: the thing (as for)
notes: A contraction of 것 (geot, thing) and the topic particle 은 (eun). This construction is extremely common in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 별로
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not really, not much
notes: This adverb is used with negative expressions like 없다 (eopda), 안 (an), or 못 (mot) to mean 'not particularly' or 'not much'.
--- word ---
term: 없어
type: Adjective
breakdown: 없다 + ~어
meaning: there isn't
notes: This is the informal, non-polite conjugation of 없다 (eopda). This `~아/어` ending is characteristic of 반말 (banmal), the casual speech style Hyejin is using with her fans.

=== [7:23] 근데 하필 또 과자를 사가지고 ===
meaning: But of all things, I had to go and buy snacks...
tone: Informal, explanatory, trailing off to imply a consequence.
--- word ---
term: 근데
type: Conjunction (Contracted)
breakdown: 그런데
meaning: but, however
notes: A very common contraction of 그런데 (geureonde). It's used frequently in spoken Korean to mean 'but' or to change the subject.
--- word ---
term: 하필
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: of all things
notes: This adverb adds a nuance of 'why at this particular time?' or 'why this specific thing?'. It implies that the choice or timing was inconvenient, unfortunate, or unexpected. Here, it suggests that buying snacks was a poor choice.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: again, also
notes: Here, it adds emphasis, like 'on top of everything else'.
--- word ---
term: 과자를
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 과자 + ~를
meaning: snacks (as object)
notes: 과자 (gwaja) means 'snacks' or 'cookies'. ~를 (reul) is the object particle, marking 'snacks' as the direct object of the verb 'to buy'.
--- word ---
term: 사가지고
type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 사다 + ~아 가지고
meaning: bought, and so...
notes: The `~아/어 가지고` ending is a colloquial connector. It can indicate a sequence of actions ('I bought it and then...') or a reason ('Because I bought it...'). Here, it functions as a reason for the implied problem that follows.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~아/어 가지고
meaning: A colloquial grammar pattern used to connect clauses. It can mean 'and then' (sequence) or 'because' (reason). It is primarily used in spoken Korean.
example: 배고파 가지고 밥 먹었어요. (I was hungry, so I ate.)
level: Intermediate

=== [7:26] 과자는 뭔지 알지? ===
meaning: Snacks, you know what they're like, right?

term: 과자는
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 과자 + ~는
meaning: as for snacks
notes: The topic marker ~는 (neun) is used here to single out 'snacks' as the topic of the sentence, implying a contrast or special focus on them.
--- word ---
term: 뭔지

breakdown: 무엇 + ~인지
meaning: what it is
notes: A contraction of 뭐 (mwo, 'what') and the ending ~인지 (inji), which is used in embedded questions. The full form is 무엇인지 (mueosinji).
--- word ---
term: 알지?
type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 알다 + ~지?
meaning: you know, right?
notes: The ending ~지? (ji?) is a tag question used to confirm something the speaker believes the listener also knows. It creates a friendly, inclusive tone. The Story Bible notes that '뭔지 알지?' is a frequent verbal tic for this speaker.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Interrogative + ~(이)ㄴ지 알다/모르다
meaning: Used to embed a question within a larger sentence, meaning 'to know/not know what/who/when/where...'.
example: 이게 뭔지 알아요? (Do you know what this is?)
level: Intermediate

=== [7:28] 부피만 차는 거 ===
meaning: (They're) the thing that only takes up space.
tone: Informal, descriptive, finishing the previous thought.
--- word ---
term: 부피만
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 부피 + ~만
meaning: only volume/space
notes: 부피 (bupi) means 'volume' or 'bulk'. The particle ~만 (man) means 'only' or 'just', emphasizing that snacks take up space and not much else (like nutritional value).
--- word ---
term: 차는
type: Verb (Adjective Form)
breakdown: 차다 + ~는
meaning: that takes up
notes: This is the present-tense adjective form of 차다 (chada). In '가득 찼더라고', it meant 'to be full', but here it means 'to occupy' or 'to take up' space. The ~는 (neun) ending turns the verb into an adjective describing the following noun.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Pronoun (Contracted)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing
notes: A very common colloquial contraction of the pronoun 것 (geot), meaning 'thing'. The full phrase would be '부피만 차는 것'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~는 것
meaning: A grammar pattern that turns a present-tense verb phrase into a noun clause, meaning 'the act of V-ing' or 'the thing that V-s'.
example: 제가 좋아하는 것은 영화 보기예요. (The thing I like is watching movies.)
level: Beginner

type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Ah, oh
notes: A common interjection used to express a wide range of emotions like surprise, realization, frustration, or pain. Here, it conveys a sense of mild exasperation or worry.
--- word ---
term: 이거
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 이것
meaning: this thing
notes: A very common colloquial contraction of '이것' (this thing). In spoken Korean, '이거' (this), '그거' (that), and '저거' (that over there) are used far more frequently than their full forms.
--- word ---
term: 어떻게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 어떻다 + 게
meaning: how
notes: Derived from the descriptive verb '어떻다' (to be how). While it literally means 'how', it's often used to express a sense of helplessness or to ask for a solution, as in '어떻게 하지?' (What should I do?).
--- word ---
term: 가져가?
type: Verb
breakdown: 가져가다
meaning: take/carry?
notes: The base verb is '가져가다' (to take/carry something away). This is the informal, non-polite question form (반말, banmal). The polite equivalent would be '가져가요?'.

--- word ---
term: 이거
type: Pronoun

breakdown: 이것

--- word ---

breakdown: 어떻다 + 게

meaning: take/carry
