meaning: standard, criteria
notes: Refers to a standard or benchmark for judgment. 기준이 높다 (standards are high) is a common phrase.
--- word ---
term: 높아
type: adjective
breakdown: 높다 + -아
meaning: is high
notes: The informal, present tense conjugation of 높다 (to be high). It can refer to physical height or abstract levels like standards.

=== [24:45] 너희들 그걸 알아야 돼 내가 얼마나 간별하고 간별하고 간별해서 너희들한테 소개해 주는 건지 ===
meaning: You all have to know this: how much I sorted through, sorted through, and sorted through to introduce these to you.
tone: Informal, emphatic, playfully boasting to her fans.
--- word ---
term: 너희들
type: pronoun
breakdown: 너 + 희 + 들
meaning: you all, you guys
notes: An informal way to say 'you' (plural). It's used when speaking to a group of people who are friends, the same age, or younger. The speaker (Hyejin) uses this to address her fans (Cheong-i) in a friendly, familiar way.
--- word ---
term: 그걸
type: pronoun + particle (contraction)
breakdown: 그것 + 을
meaning: that thing
notes: A common contraction of 그것 (that thing) and the object particle 을. Used frequently in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 알아야
type: verb
breakdown: 알다 + -아야
meaning: have to know
notes: Part of the -아야/어야 되다/하다 grammar pattern, which expresses obligation or necessity. The full form would be 알아야 돼.
--- word ---
term: 돼
type: verb
breakdown: 되다 + -어
meaning: must, have to
notes: The second part of the -아야/어야 되다 pattern. A common mistake for learners is confusing 돼 (되어) with 되 (the stem). In a sentence ending, it must be 돼.
--- word ---
term: 내가
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 + 가
meaning: I
notes: The informal pronoun 나 (I) combined with the subject particle 가. Used to emphasize the speaker as the one performing the action.
--- word ---
term: 얼마나
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: how much
notes: An interrogative adverb used to ask 'how much' or 'to what extent'. It can be used in questions or, as in this case, in an embedded clause.
--- word ---
term: 간별하고
type: verb
breakdown: 간별하다 + -고
meaning: sort out and...
notes: 간별하다 means 'to discern' or 'to select carefully'. The -고 ending connects actions in a sequence. The repetition of this word is for emphasis, highlighting how carefully she chose the snacks.
--- word ---
term: 간별해서
type: verb
breakdown: 간별하다 + -해서
meaning: after sorting out
notes: The -아/어서 ending indicates a cause-and-effect or sequential relationship. 'I sorted them out, and as a result...'.
--- word ---
term: 너희들한테
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 너희들 + 한테
meaning: to you all
notes: 한테 is a colloquial particle meaning 'to' or 'from' someone. It's the spoken equivalent of the more formal/written particle 에게.
--- word ---
term: 소개해
type: verb
breakdown: 소개하다 + -해
meaning: introduce
notes: The informal conjugation of 소개하다 (to introduce). Here it's part of the larger '소개해 주는' construction.
--- word ---
term: 주는
type: verb (adjective form)
breakdown: 주다 + -는
meaning: that (I) do for you
notes: From the verb 주다 (to give). When attached to another verb with -아/어, it means to do that action for someone's benefit. 소개해 주다 means 'to introduce (for you)'.
--- word ---
term: 건지
type: noun + verb (contraction)
breakdown: 것 + 인지
meaning: whether it is the thing that...
notes: A contraction of 것인지. The -(으)ㄴ/는지 grammar pattern is used to embed a question or point of uncertainty into a sentence. The whole clause '얼마나...주는 건지' acts as the object of 알아야 돼.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -아야/어야 되다/하다
meaning: Expresses obligation or necessity, equivalent to 'must' or 'have to' in English.
example: 지금 가야 돼요. (I have to go now.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -아/어 주다
meaning: Indicates that an action is performed for someone else's benefit. It adds a sense of favor or politeness.
example: 문을 열어 주세요. (Please open the door for me.)
level: Beginner

=== [24:54] 그동안의 그 과자들 이거는 케이크에 아 그 설탕 케이크 맛이야 겉에 그 설탕 굳어있는 그 그걸 쿠키로 만든 느낌 ===

meaning: that, those

breakdown: 과자 + 들

notes: 과자 means 'snack' or 'confectionery'. The suffix 들 is the plural marker, though it's often omitted in Korean unless plurality needs to be emphasized.
--- word ---
term: 이거는

breakdown: 이것 + 은
meaning: as for this one
notes: A contraction of 이것은. The topic particle 은/는 is used to single out 'this one' for discussion, contrasting it with the others.
--- word ---
term: 설탕
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: sugar
notes: The Korean word for sugar.
--- word ---
term: 케이크
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: cake
notes: A loanword from English, pronounced 'ke-i-keu'.

meaning: it's the taste of...
notes: 맛 means 'taste' or 'flavor'. It's combined with the informal ending of 이다 (to be). 'N + 맛이다' means 'it tastes like N'.
--- word ---
term: 겉에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 겉 + 에
meaning: on the outside
notes: 겉 means 'outer surface' or 'exterior'. The particle 에 marks the location 'on' the surface.
--- word ---
term: 굳어있는
type: verb (adjective form)
breakdown: 굳다 + -어 있다 + -는
meaning: that is hardened
notes: This combines two grammar points. 굳다 is 'to harden'. V + -어/아 있다 describes a continuing state resulting from a past action. So, 굳어있다 means 'to be in a hardened state'. The -는 makes it an adjective modifying 'sugar'.
--- word ---
term: 그걸
type: pronoun + particle (contraction)
breakdown: 그것 + 을
meaning: that thing
notes: Contraction of 그것 (that thing) + 을 (object particle). Refers to the 'hardened sugar'.
--- word ---
term: 쿠키로
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 쿠키 + -(으)로
meaning: into a cookie
notes: The particle -(으)로 can indicate the means, material, or result of a transformation. Here, it means the sugar was transformed 'into' a cookie.
--- word ---
term: 만든
type: verb (adjective form)
breakdown: 만들다 + -ㄴ
meaning: that was made
notes: The past-tense adjective form of 만들다 (to make). It modifies the noun 느낌 (feeling).
--- word ---
term: 느낌
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: feeling, impression
notes: Ending a sentence with 느낌 is a very common and colloquial way to describe a vibe, impression, or feeling about something, similar to saying 'It has a... vibe' in English.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -어/아 있다
meaning: Expresses a state that continues after an action is completed. It's often used with verbs that imply a change of state.
example: 창문이 열려 있어요. (The window is open / in an opened state.)
level: Intermediate

=== [25:26] 왜 이렇게 했을까 음 핫 허니바콘 완전 맛있지 그래 꽈이 꽈이도 미쳤지 ===

meaning: it's insane, right?

--- grammar ---

meaning: A sentence ending used to confirm information or seek agreement from the listener. It implies the speaker expects a 'yes' answer.

=== [25:53] 뭐 이거는 너무 유명하지만 이건 하나씩 꺼내먹을 땐 너무 기분이 좋아 ===

--- word ---
term: 뭐

breakdown: 
meaning: well...
notes: Used as a filler word at the beginning of a sentence, similar to 'well...' or 'I mean...'. It can soften the statement or indicate the speaker is gathering their thoughts.
--- word ---
term: 이거는
type: pronoun + particle (contraction)
breakdown: 이것 + 은
meaning: as for this
notes: Contraction of 이것은. The topic particle 은 highlights 'this thing' as the subject of the comment.
--- word ---
term: 너무

breakdown:

notes: An adverb that means 'too' (as in 'too much'), but is very commonly used in conversation to mean 'very' or 'really'.

--- word ---

--- word ---
term: 하나씩

breakdown: 하나 + -씩

--- word ---
term: 좋아
type: adjective
breakdown: 좋다 + -아
meaning: is good
notes: The informal, present tense conjugation of 좋다 (to be good).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)ㄹ 때
meaning: Indicates the time when an action or state occurs. It translates to 'when...' or 'at the time of...'
example: 공부할 때 음악을 들어요. (I listen to music when I study.)
level: Beginner

=== [26:11] 아 이거 소분이 아니었네 ===
meaning: Ah, this wasn't individually packaged.
tone: Informal, expressing mild surprise or realization.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: ah, oh
notes: A common interjection used to express surprise, realization, or understanding.
--- word ---
term: 이거
type: pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: this
notes: The colloquial form of the pronoun 이것 (this thing).
--- word ---
term: 소분이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 소분 + 이
meaning: individual packaging
notes: 소분 (sobun) literally means 'small division' and refers to the practice of dividing a large product into smaller portions, i.e., individual packaging.
--- word ---
term: 아니었네
type: verb
breakdown: 아니다 + -었- + -네
meaning: it wasn't...!
notes: This combines 아니다 (to not be), the past tense marker -었-, and the sentence ending -네. The -네 ending expresses the speaker's mild surprise or realization upon discovering a new piece of information.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -네(요)
meaning: A sentence ending used to express mild surprise, admiration, or realization about something the speaker has just learned or noticed.
example: 이 케이크 정말 맛있네요! (Wow, this cake is really delicious!)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + 이/가 아니다
meaning: The negative form of 'to be N'. It is used to state that something is not a particular noun.
example: 저는 학생이 아니에요. (I am not a student.)
level: Beginner

=== [26:50] 코알라 노마치라는 과자인데 짱구 원장에서 초코비 원정으로 유명하다던데 ===
meaning: It's a snack called 'Koala no Machi', and I heard it's famous as the original Chocobi from Crayon Shin-chan.
tone: Informal, relaying information from a fan's comment.
--- word ---
term: 코알라 노마치라는
type: Noun phrase
breakdown: 코알라 노마치 (Koala no Machi) + -(이)라는 (called)
meaning: called 'Koala no Machi'
notes: The speaker is reading the Japanese product name 'コアラのマーチ' (Koala's March) as '코알라 노마치'. -(이)라는 is a contraction of -(이)라고 하는 and is used to quote or define a noun. It means 'a (noun) called (name)'.
--- word ---
term: 과자인데
type: Noun + verb ending
breakdown: 과자 (snack) + -인데 (is... and/but)
meaning: it's a snack and/but
notes: The -ㄴ/는데 ending provides background information for the next clause. It can be translated as 'and', 'but', or simply connect two related thoughts. It's extremely common in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 짱구
type: Proper noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Jjanggu
notes: 짱구는 못말려 (Jjanggu-neun Motmalyeo) is the Korean title for the popular Japanese anime and manga 'Crayon Shin-chan'.
--- word ---
term: 원장에서
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 원장 (director) + -에서 (in/from)
meaning: from the director
notes: This is likely a typo in the fan's comment or a misreading by the speaker. The intended word was probably 원작에서 (wonjag-eseo), meaning 'in the original work'.
--- word ---
term: 초코비
type: Proper noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Chocobi
notes: A fictional chocolate snack that the main character in 'Crayon Shin-chan' loves. It was later commercialized as a real product.
--- word ---
term: 원정으로
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 원정 (expedition) + -으로 (as/with)
meaning: as the expedition
notes: This is almost certainly a typo for 원조로 (wonjo-ro), meaning 'as the original' or 'as the originator'. This kind of typo is common in fast-paced online chats.
--- word ---
term: 유명하다던데
type: Verb + verb ending
breakdown: 유명하다 (to be famous) + -다던데 (I heard that...)
meaning: I heard it's famous
notes: This is a reported speech pattern. It combines -다고 하다 (to say that...) and -던데 (recalling/providing background). It's used to relay something you heard from someone else, often with a nuance of expecting a reaction or leading to another thought.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + -(이)라는 + Noun
meaning: A pattern used to introduce or define something. It means 'a (Noun 2) called (Noun 1)'.
example: 서울이라는 도시 (Seoul-iraneun dosi) - 'a city called Seoul'
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb/Adjective + -다던데
meaning: Expresses something the speaker heard from another source. It implies that the information is secondhand. Translates to 'I heard that...' or 'They say that...'.
example: 그 식당이 맛있다던데 가 볼까요? (Geu sikdang-i masitdadeonde ga bolkkayo?) - 'I heard that restaurant is good, shall we go?'
level: Intermediate

=== [28:24] 이거는 저와 함께 갑니다 ===

=== [28:40] 우와 진짜 밤이야 너무 신기한데 ===
meaning: Wow, it's a real chestnut. This is so fascinating.
tone: Informal, surprised, impressed. Reacting to the snack's appearance.
--- word ---
term: 우와
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Wow!
notes: An exclamation of surprise, wonder, or amazement. Very common in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb / Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: really, real
notes: A versatile word used as an adverb ('really') or a noun ('the real thing'). Here it modifies '밤' (chestnut), meaning 'a real chestnut'.
--- word ---
term: 밤이야
type: Noun + Copula
breakdown: 밤 + 이야
meaning: it's a chestnut
notes: The informal ending for '이다' (ida, to be) when attached to a noun ending in a consonant. '밤' (bam) + '이야' (iya). The polite form would be '밤이에요' (bam-ieyo).
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, very, too
notes: An adverb of degree. While it traditionally meant 'too' in a negative sense, in modern colloquial Korean, it's frequently used to mean 'very' or 'so' in positive contexts, like here.
--- word ---
term: 신기한데
type: Adjective + Ending
breakdown: 신기하다 + ~(으)ㄴ데
meaning: it's fascinating/cool, but/and...
notes: The '~ㄴ데' (nde) ending is attached to the adjective '신기하다' (singihada, to be fascinating). It's used to provide background information, express surprise, or trail off, leaving the sentence open-ended.
--- grammar ---
pattern: A/V + ~(으)ㄴ/는데
meaning: A sentence ending that provides background context for what comes next, shows a soft contrast, or expresses surprise. It's less direct than endings like '~서' (so) or '~지만' (but).
example: 비가 오는데 어디 가? (It's raining, so where are you going?)
level: Intermediate

=== [28:50] 음 맛있는데 뭐야 ===

term: 맛있는데

breakdown: 맛있다 + ~는데
meaning: it's delicious, and...
notes: The '~는데' (neunde) ending on '맛있다' (masitda, to be delicious) implies that the speaker is stating a fact ('it's delicious') while also having a follow-up thought or question.
--- word ---
term: 뭐야
type: Pronoun + Copula
breakdown: 뭐 + 야
meaning: What is it?
notes: The informal, casual way to ask 'What is it?'. '뭐' (mwo) means 'what' and '야' (ya) is the informal copula 'to be'. The polite equivalent is '뭐예요?' (mwoyeyo?).

meaning: it's delicious...

=== [29:57] 아 이거 좀 많이 사가야겠는데 ===

--- word ---

breakdown: 이것 (contraction)
meaning: this

--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a bit, some
notes: A contraction of '조금' (jogeum). While it means 'a little', it's often used to make statements or requests sound softer and less demanding.
--- word ---
term: 많이

breakdown: 많다 + ~이
meaning: a lot, many
notes: The adverbial form of the adjective '많다' (manta, to be many/much).

meaning: I guess I should buy and take...
notes: A complex verb form. '사가다' (sada + gada) means 'to buy and take'. '~아야겠다' expresses intention or necessity ('I should'). '~는데' softens the statement, making it sound like a thought-in-progress.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~아야/어야겠다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention, will, or realization that they must or should do something. It's often used when making a decision on the spot.
example: 늦어서 뛰어가야겠다. (I'm late, so I'd better run.)

meaning: Expresses that something seems like, looks like, or is similar to the preceding noun.

=== [30:20] 씹으면 씹을수록 밤맛이 막 올라와 ===
meaning: The more you chew, the more the chestnut flavor comes out.
tone: Informal, enthusiastic, descriptive.
--- word ---
term: 씹으면
type: Verb + conditional ending
breakdown: 씹다 + -(으)면
meaning: if/when you chew
notes: 씹다 means 'to chew'. The -(으)면 ending is a common conditional marker meaning 'if' or 'when'. It attaches to the verb stem. If the stem ends in a consonant (like 씹-), -으면 is used. If it ends in a vowel, -면 is used (e.g., 가면 - if you go).
--- word ---
term: 씹을수록
type: Verb + grammar pattern
breakdown: 씹다 + -(으)ㄹ수록
meaning: the more you chew
notes: This is part of the 'V-(으)ㄹ수록' grammar pattern, which means 'the more one does V...'. It indicates that the action in the first clause leads to a corresponding increase in the state or action of the second clause.
--- word ---
term: 밤맛이
type: Noun + subject particle
breakdown: 밤맛 + -이
meaning: the chestnut flavor
notes: 밤맛 is a compound noun from 밤 (chestnut) and 맛 (taste/flavor). The particle -이 marks it as the subject of the verb. -이 is used after nouns ending in a consonant, while -가 is used after nouns ending in a vowel (e.g., 과자가 - the snack).
--- word ---
term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: intensely, just
notes: 막 is a very common colloquial adverb that adds emphasis. Here, it conveys that the flavor comes out strongly and spontaneously. It can have various meanings like 'just' (막 도착했어 - I just arrived) or 'carelessly' (돈을 막 쓰다 - to spend money recklessly).
--- word ---
term: 올라와
type: Verb
breakdown: 올라오다 -> 올라와요 -> 올라와
meaning: comes up, emerges
notes: The base verb is 올라오다 (to come up). This is conjugated in the informal, non-polite style (반말, banmal). The polite form is 올라와요. It's often used metaphorically for flavors 'coming through' or feelings 'surfacing'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄹ수록
meaning: Indicates that 'the more' an action is performed, 'the more' a certain result occurs. It connects two clauses where the second is a direct result of the increasing action in the first.
example: 한국어는 공부할수록 재미있어요. (The more you study Korean, the more interesting it is.)
level: Intermediate

=== [30:26] 밤을 동결건조시킨 듯한 느낌 ===
meaning: It feels like they freeze-dried chestnuts.
tone: Informal, descriptive, speculative.
--- word ---
term: 밤을
type: Noun + object particle
breakdown: 밤 + -을
meaning: chestnuts (as an object)
notes: 밤 means 'chestnut'. The particle -을 marks it as the direct object of the verb (동결건조시킨). -을 is used after a noun ending in a consonant, while -를 is used after a noun ending in a vowel (e.g., 사과를 - an apple).
--- word ---
term: 동결건조시킨
type: Verb (past tense modifier)
breakdown: 동결건조시키다 + -(으)ㄴ
meaning: that was freeze-dried
notes: 동결건조시키다 means 'to cause to be freeze-dried'. The -(으)ㄴ ending transforms a verb into a past-tense adjective that can modify a noun. For example, 먹은 음식 means 'the food that (I) ate'.
--- word ---
term: 듯한
type: Grammar pattern (modifier form)
breakdown: -(으)ㄴ/는 듯하다 -> -(으)ㄴ/는 듯한
meaning: that seems like / that is like
notes: This is the modifier form of the grammar pattern '-(으)ㄴ/는 듯하다', which expresses a guess or impression. The full sentence would be '동결건조시킨 듯한 느낌이다' (It is a feeling as if they freeze-dried it), but ending on the noun is common in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 느낌
type: Noun
breakdown: 느끼다 + -ㅁ
meaning: a feeling, an impression
notes: 느낌 is a noun derived from the verb 느끼다 (to feel). The -ㅁ/음 ending is a common way to nominalize verbs and adjectives. This sentence is a fragment ending with a noun, a pattern frequently used in casual, spoken Korean for effect.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄴ/는 듯하다
meaning: Expresses a guess, assumption, or impression about a situation, similar to 'it seems like' or 'it looks as if'.
example: 밖에 비가 오는 듯해요. (It seems like it's raining outside.)
level: Intermediate

meaning: as for this
notes: 이건 is a contraction of 이것 (this thing) + -은 (topic particle). The topic particle -은/는 introduces a topic or shows contrast. Here, it emphasizes 'this snack' as the specific subject of her personal opinion.

type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, simply
notes: A versatile adverb meaning 'just' (그냥 해 - just do it) or 'for no particular reason'. Here, it acts as a filler to soften the statement and make it sound more spontaneous.

meaning: personally
notes: The particle -(으)로 attaches to nouns to create adverbs. When combined with -적 nouns, it forms adverbs like 개인적으로 (personally) or 공식적으로 (officially).

--- word ---
term: 너무

=== [31:19] 응 이거 다 동키호텔에서 산 거야 ===

--- word ---

breakdown:

--- word ---
term: 이거
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 이것
meaning: This thing / This
notes: A contraction of 이것 (igeot). In spoken Korean, contractions like 이거 (this), 그거 (that), and 저거 (that over there) are far more common than their full forms.
--- word ---
term: 다
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: All / Everything
notes: A versatile adverb that can mean 'all,' 'completely,' or 'entirely.' It's placed before the verb it modifies.

breakdown: 동키호텔 + 에서
meaning: At/from Donki-hotel
notes: The particle 에서 (eseo) marks the location where an action takes place. The speaker is humorously mispronouncing the store name 'Don Quijote' (돈키호테) as 'Donki-hotel,' a personal quirk noted in the context.
--- word ---

notes: A descriptive verb literally meaning 'taste exists.' Using the dictionary form as a complete sentence is a common feature of casual, exclamatory speech (반말, banmal). The polite form is 맛있어요 (masisseoyo).

=== [31:43] 이거는 갈 때 좀 이거를 이제 사가야겠다 앞에 선물용으로 ===
meaning: I should buy some of this as a gift for people back home when I leave.
tone: Informal, thoughtful, making a mental note.
--- word ---
term: 이거는
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 이거 + -는
meaning: As for this...
notes: The topic marker -는 (neun) highlights 'this thing' (이거), distinguishing it from other items and making it the focus of her thought.
--- word ---
term: 갈
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 가다 + -ㄹ
meaning: (time) to go
notes: The future adnominal form of 가다 (gada, 'to go'), which modifies the following noun 때 (ttae, 'time').
--- word ---
term: 때
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: when
notes: Combined with a preceding verb in its modifier form (V-ㄹ/을 때), it means 'when [verb-ing]'. For example, '먹을 때' (meogeul ttae) means 'when eating'.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금
meaning: some / a bit
notes: A contraction of 조금 (jogeum). It's often used to soften a statement or, as in this case, to mean 'some' or 'a few'.
--- word ---
term: 이거를
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 이거 + -를
meaning: this thing (as object)
notes: The object marker -를 (reul) indicates that 'this thing' is the direct object of the verb 'to buy' (사가야겠다).
--- word ---
term: 이제
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now
notes: 이제 (ije) refers to a point in time starting from now and moving forward, often implying a change of state or decision. It differs from 지금 (jigeum), which means 'right now' at this exact moment.
--- word ---
term: 사가야겠다
type: Verb
breakdown: 사다 + 가다 + -아야겠다
meaning: I should buy and take
notes: A compound verb 사가다 (sagada, 'to buy and take with you') combined with the grammar pattern -아야겠다 (-ayagetda), which expresses the speaker's realization or intention that they 'must' or 'should' do something.
--- word ---
term: 앞에
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 앞 + -에
meaning: for people back home
notes: Literally 'in front of' or 'to the front.' Here it's used colloquially to refer to the people at her next destination or back home, the ones she will be 'going to'.
--- word ---
term: 선물용으로
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 선물 + -용 + -으로
meaning: as a gift
notes: The suffix -용 (-yong) means 'for the use of'. The particle -(으)로 (-euro) indicates purpose. Together, 선물용으로 (seonmullyongeuro) means 'for the purpose of a gift'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아야/어야겠다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or realization that they should or must do something. It's often used for decisions made on the spot.
example: 비가 오네. 우산을 가져가야겠다. (Oh, it's raining. I should take an umbrella.)
level: Intermediate

=== [32:13] 너희들도 꼭 먹어봐 ===

--- word ---

meaning: definitely, surely

--- grammar ---

meaning: To express trying or experiencing an action. When used as an informal command (V-아/어 봐), it works as a friendly suggestion.

=== [32:23] 목이 맥힌다 근데 ===
meaning: But it's making my throat feel clogged.

term: 목이

breakdown: 목 (throat) + 이 (subject particle)
meaning: the throat (is)
notes: 목 means 'throat' or 'neck'. The particle 이 marks it as the subject of the verb. The subject particle 이 is used after nouns ending in a consonant, while 가 is used after vowels.

type: verb
breakdown: 막히다 (to be blocked) + -ㄴ다 (plain form ending)

--- word ---
term: 근데
type: conjunction
breakdown: 그런데 (but/however) contraction
meaning: but, however
notes: 근데 is a very common contraction of 그런데. It's used ubiquitously in spoken Korean to connect contrasting ideas or to change the subject, similar to 'but' or 'by the way' in English.

=== [32:37] 이 밤칩은 어떤 느낌인지 알겠어? ===
meaning: Do you get what kind of feeling these chestnut chips have?
tone: Informal, engaging, trying to explain a sensation.
--- word ---
term: 이
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: this
notes: The determiner 이 is used for things that are close to the speaker. It corresponds to 'this' or 'these'. Compare with 그 (that, close to the listener) and 저 (that, far from both).
--- word ---
term: 밤칩은
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 밤칩 (chestnut chip) + 은 (topic particle)
meaning: as for these chestnut chips
notes: The particle 은/는 marks the topic of the sentence—what the speaker is talking about. It can create a slight nuance of contrast. 은 is used after a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 어떤
type: determiner/adjective
breakdown: 어떻다 (to be how) + -ㄴ (modifier form)
meaning: what kind of
notes: 어떤 is used before a noun to ask about its characteristics or nature, translating to 'what kind of' or 'which'.
--- word ---
term: 느낌인지
type: noun + verb ending
breakdown: 느낌 (feeling) + 이다 (to be) + -ㄴ지 (indirect question)
meaning: what feeling it is
notes: The grammar pattern -(으)ㄴ지 embeds a question or point of uncertainty into a larger sentence. Here, '어떤 느낌이다' (it is a certain kind of feeling) becomes '어떤 느낌인지' (what kind of feeling it is).
--- word ---
term: 알겠어?
type: verb
breakdown: 알다 (to know) + -겠- (conjecture) + -어? (question ending)
meaning: Do you get it?
notes: While -겠- can indicate future tense or intention, in 알겠다 it often expresses a present understanding or realization ('I get it'). As a question, 알겠어? is a common way to ask 'Do you understand?' or 'You get what I mean?', often softer than asking 알아?.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun/Verb/Adj. + -(으)ㄴ/는지
meaning: An ending used to embed a question (who, what, where, when, how, whether) into another clause.
example: 이게 얼마인지 모르겠어요. (I don't know how much this is.)
level: Intermediate

=== [32:41] 너무 맛있어서 ===

type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, very
notes: Historically, 너무 meant 'too much' and had a negative connotation. However, in modern spoken Korean, it is widely used as a general intensifier for both positive and negative situations, like 'very' or 'so'.
--- word ---
term: 맛있어서

meaning: because it's delicious

meaning: Connects two clauses to show a cause-and-effect relationship. It translates to 'so', 'because', or 'and then'.
example: 비가 와서 길이 막혀요. (Because it's raining, the roads are congested.)

=== [33:07] 물 가지러 간 건데 이게 보여가지고 ===

term: 간
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 가다 + ~ㄴ
meaning: that went
notes: This is the past tense adjective form of the verb '가다' (to go), modifying the noun '것' (thing) which is part of '건데'.
--- word ---

type: Noun + Verb Ending
breakdown: 것 + ~인데
meaning: it was the thing that... but
notes: This is a contraction of '것인데'. The '~ㄴ/은/는데' ending is extremely common in spoken Korean to provide background context before stating the main point. It can mean 'but,' 'and,' or 'so,' depending on the context.

type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 이것 + ~이
meaning: this (thing)

meaning: I saw it, and so...
notes: '보이다' is the passive form of '보다' (to see), meaning 'to be seen' or 'to be visible.' The '~아/어 가지고' pattern is a very common spoken alternative to '~아/어서,' meaning 'because' or indicating a sequence of actions ('and then').
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 가지고
meaning: A spoken grammar pattern indicating reason ('because') or a sequence of actions ('and then'). It's often used as a more conversational alternative to V-아/어서.
example: 배고파 가지고 빵을 샀어요. (Because I was hungry, I bought bread.)

=== [33:17] 요거트가 먹고 싶었거든 ===

meaning: I wanted to, you see
notes: The '~거든' ending is used in casual conversation (반말, banmal) to provide a reason or background information for a previous statement or action. It implies, '...and that's why.' The past tense '~었~' indicates the desire was in the past.

pattern: V-고 싶다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's desire or wish to do something. 'I want to V.'

example: 왜 늦었어요? 길이 많이 막혔거든요. (Why were you late? Because the traffic was really bad.)

breakdown: 
meaning: today
notes:

term: 완전

breakdown: 
meaning: completely / totally
notes: In colloquial speech, '완전' is frequently used like the English slang 'totally' or 'so' to emphasize the word that follows. For example, '완전 맛있어!' (It's totally delicious!).

breakdown: 먹방 + 이다
meaning: it is a mukbang
notes: '먹방' (meokbang) is a Korean portmanteau of '먹는 방송' (meokneun bangsong), meaning 'eating broadcast.' It's a global phenomenon that originated in Korea. '이다' is the 'to be' verb in its plain, declarative form.

=== [33:32] 배고파 ===
meaning: I'm hungry.
tone: Informal, simple statement of fact.
--- word ---
term: 배고파
type: Adjective
breakdown: 배고프다
meaning: I'm hungry
notes: This is the informal, non-polite (반말, banmal) conjugation of '배고프다' (to be hungry). It's an example of a 'ㅡ' irregular verb: when adding an ending that starts with '아/어', the 'ㅡ' is dropped, and '아' or '어' is chosen based on the preceding vowel. Since '고' has an 'ㅗ' vowel, '아' is added: 배고프 + 아 -> 배고파.

meaning: Hmm / Mmm

term: 먹다
type: Verb
breakdown: 먹다
meaning: to eat
notes: This is the dictionary form. Here it's part of the common phrase '먹다 남은', which means 'leftover from eating'.
--- word ---
term: 남은
type: Verb (adjective form)
breakdown: 남다 + -(으)ㄴ
meaning: leftover / remaining
notes: This is the past tense adjectival form of the verb 남다 (to remain). The -(으)ㄴ ending modifies the following noun (팬케이크), describing it as something that 'was left over'.
--- word ---
term: 팬케이크
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: pancake
notes: A loanword from English, pronounced 'paen-ke-i-keu'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb + -(으)ㄴ + Noun
meaning: A past-tense adjectival clause. It modifies a noun by describing an action that has already been completed.
example: 어제 본 영화 (The movie I saw yesterday).

=== [33:42] 아 이따 조식 먹으려고 그랬는데 ===

--- word ---
term: 아

breakdown: 
meaning: Ah / Oh
notes: A common exclamation expressing realization, surprise, or mild frustration.
--- word ---
term: 이따
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a little later
notes: A common contraction of 이따가. It refers to a time later on the same day. Don't confuse it with 있다가 (from 있다, to exist), which means 'after being/staying for a bit'.
--- word ---
term: 조식
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: breakfast
notes: A Sino-Korean word (朝食) often used in contexts like hotels or official schedules. The more common, everyday word for breakfast is 아침.
--- word ---
term: 먹으려고
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 먹다 + -(으)려고
meaning: in order to eat / intending to eat
notes: This is part of the -(으)려고 하다 grammar pattern, which expresses the speaker's intention or plan to do something.
--- word ---
term: 그랬는데
type: Verb + Conjunction
breakdown: 그렇다 + -았/었- + -는데
meaning: I was going to, but...
notes: Refers back to the intention '먹으려고' (planning to eat). The -는데 ending provides background information and often implies a contrast or an unexpected result, leaving the sentence unfinished.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)려고 하다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or plan to do something. The 하다 part is often implied or replaced with another verb like 그랬다.
example: 한국에 가려고 돈을 모으고 있어요. (I'm saving money to go to Korea.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데
meaning: A connective ending that provides background context for the following clause. It can also show contrast (like 'but') or leave a sentence unfinished to imply a following thought.
example: 비가 오는데, 우산 있어요? (It's raining, do you have an umbrella?)
level: Intermediate

=== [33:45] 이러면 배불러서 못 먹는데 ===
meaning: If I do this, I'll be too full to eat it...
tone: Informal, worried, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 이러면
type: Verb + Conjunction
breakdown: 이렇다 + -(으)면
meaning: If it's like this / If I do this
notes: A contraction of 이렇게 하면 (if I do it like this). 이렇다 means 'to be like this'. The -(으)면 ending creates a conditional 'if' clause.
--- word ---
term: 배불러서
type: Adjective + Conjunction
breakdown: 배부르다 + -아/어서
meaning: because I'm full
notes: 배부르다 is the adjective 'to be full'. The -아/어서 ending indicates a cause-and-effect relationship. Because I'm full, [something happens].
--- word ---
term: 못
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: cannot
notes: This adverb is placed before a verb to indicate inability. It's different from 안, which indicates unwillingness. '못 먹어' means 'I can't eat', while '안 먹어' means 'I don't/won't eat'.
--- word ---
term: 먹는데
type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 먹다 + -는데
meaning: ...can eat, but...
notes: The verb 먹다 (to eat) combined with the sentence-ending -는데. It trails off, implying a consequence like 'I won't be able to eat breakfast'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 못 + Verb
meaning: Expresses inability to do something, as opposed to simply not doing it (which is expressed with 안 + Verb).
example: 매운 음식을 못 먹어요. (I can't eat spicy food.)
level: Beginner

=== [34:19] 너무 지저분하게 먹었다 ===

--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too / so / very

breakdown: 먹다 + -았/었- + -다
meaning: ate
notes: The past tense of 먹다 (to eat). The -다 ending here is a declarative sentence ender used in casual speech (반말, banmal), often when talking to oneself or making a simple statement.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Adjective Stem + -게
meaning: Changes an adjective into an adverb, describing the manner in which a verb's action is carried out.
example: 예쁘게 웃어요. (She smiles prettily.)
level: Beginner

=== [34:27] 이렇게 먹을게 ===
meaning: I'll eat like this.
tone: Informal, declarative, making a decision.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + -게
meaning: like this / in this way
notes: Formed from the descriptive verb 이렇다 (to be like this) plus the adverbial ending -게. It refers to a manner or method that is being demonstrated or is clear from the context.
--- word ---
term: 먹을게
type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 먹다 + -(으)ㄹ게
meaning: I will eat
notes: The -(으)ㄹ게 ending is used in informal speech (반말, banmal) to express the speaker's intention or will. It carries a nuance of 'Okay, then I'll...' and can only be used for first-person subjects (I/we).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)ㄹ게(요)
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or a promise to do something. It's a first-person statement and often implies that the action is for the listener's benefit or is a response to the current situation. The informal form is -(으)ㄹ게.
example: 내가 먼저 갈게. (I'll go first.)
level: Beginner

=== [34:30] 그러니까 많이 먹으면 안 돼 조식 ===

type: Adverb

meaning: If (I) eat
notes: The conditional ending -(으)면 means 'if' or 'when'. It attaches to verb and adjective stems. Since 먹다's stem ends in a consonant, -으면 is used.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Not
notes: A negative adverb placed directly before a verb or adjective. This is the most common way to form short negatives in spoken Korean, as opposed to the longer -지 않다 form (e.g., 먹지 않다).
--- word ---
term: 돼
type: Verb
breakdown: 되다 + -어
meaning: it's okay
notes: The informal (반말) form of 돼요. 되다 means 'to become' or 'to be okay/permissible'. The phrase '안 돼' means 'it's not okay' or 'one shouldn't'. This is the casual form used with friends or fans.
--- word ---
term: 조식
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Breakfast
notes: A Sino-Korean word (朝食) for breakfast, often used in formal settings like hotels or restaurants. The more common, everyday word is 아침 or 아침밥. Its use here is natural as the speaker is in a hotel.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)면 안 되다
meaning: Expresses prohibition: 'one must not', 'it is not allowed to', or 'you shouldn't'.
example: 수업 시간에 자면 안 돼요. (You shouldn't sleep during class.)

=== [34:58] 순위를 매기자면 ===

type: noun + particle
breakdown: 순위 + 에
meaning: in the ranking
notes: 순위 (sunwi) means 'ranking' or 'order'. The particle 에 (e) is a location or time marker, here indicating 'in' or 'at' a certain position within the ranking.
--- word ---
term: 껴줄게
type: verb
breakdown: 끼다 + -어 주다 + -ㄹ게
meaning: I'll include you
notes: This is a combination of 끼다 (kkida - to be included, to join), the auxiliary verb -어 주다 (-eo juda - to do something for someone), and the ending -ㄹ게 (-lge - informal 'I will'). 끼어 주다 contracts to 껴주다. breakdown: -(이)라고 하는
meaning: that is called / that says
notes: This is a contraction of '-(이)라고 하는'. It's used to quote speech, thoughts, or names, and attach them to a noun. Here, it attaches the quoted thought 'I'll just include you' to the noun 'feeling' (느낌).
--- word ---
term: 느낌이야
type: noun + copula
breakdown: 느낌 + -이야
meaning: it's a feeling/vibe
notes: 느낌 (neukkim) means 'feeling' or 'vibe'. -이야 (-iya) is the informal, non-polite ending for the verb 'to be' (이다) when the noun ends in a consonant. If the noun ends in a vowel, -야 (-ya) is used.

pattern: -(으)ㄹ게
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or a promise to the listener in an informal context. It implies the action is related to or for the benefit of the listener.

--- grammar ---
pattern: -(이)라는 + Noun
meaning: A quoting particle used to describe a noun with a preceding quote, name, or phrase. It means 'a noun called/that says [quote]'.
example: '사랑'이라는 단어 (sarang-iraneun daneo) - The word 'love'.
level: Intermediate

=== [35:57] 너무 아는 맛이니까 ===

tone: Informal, explanatory.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very
notes: Traditionally, 너무 (neomu) meant 'too' in a negative sense (e.g., 'too spicy'). However, in modern colloquial Korean, it's very commonly used to mean 'very' or 'really' in both positive and negative contexts, like here.

term: 아는
type: verb (adjective form)
breakdown: 알다 + -는
meaning: that (I) know
notes: This is the present tense adjective form of the verb 알다 (alda - to know). The -는 ending attaches to action verb stems to modify a following noun. Here, 아는 modifies 맛 (mat), creating the phrase 'a taste that I know'.
--- word ---
term: 맛이니까
type: noun + copula + particle
breakdown: 맛 + -이다 + -(으)니까
meaning: because it's a flavor
notes: 맛 (mat) means 'taste/flavor'. It's combined with the copula -이다 (-ida, 'to be') and the reason-giving particle -(으)니까 (-eunikka). -(으)니까 is used to state a reason, often one that the speaker assumes the listener can understand or that leads to a suggestion or command.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 Noun
meaning: A grammar pattern used to modify a noun with an action verb in the present tense. It describes the noun as something that is performing the action.
example: 읽는 책 (ingneun chaek) - a book that (someone) is reading.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)니까
meaning: A connective ending that means 'because' or 'since'. It's often used when the reason is a subjective judgment or a discovery, and it frequently precedes a suggestion, command, or statement of intent.
example: 비가 오니까 우산을 가져가세요. (biga onikka usaneul gajyeogaseyo) - Because it's raining, please take an umbrella.
level: Beginner

=== [36:10] 나머지 과자는 내 기억에서 지우려고 ===
meaning: I'm trying to erase the rest of the snacks from my memory.
tone: Informal, humorous, making a resolution.
--- word ---
term: 나머지
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: the rest, remainder
notes: Refers to what is left over. It can be used for objects, people, or time. For example, '나머지 공부' (nam-eo-ji gong-bu) means 'after-school study' for students who need to catch up.
--- word ---
term: 과자는
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 과자 + -는
meaning: snacks (as for)
notes: The topic particle '-는' (neun) is attached to '과자' (gwaja, snack). It marks 'snacks' as the topic of the sentence, often implying a contrast with something else (e.g., the snacks she already ate).
--- word ---
term: 내
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: my
notes: This is the informal/casual possessive pronoun for 'I'. It's a contraction of '나의' (na-ui). The formal equivalent is '저의' (jeo-ui), used when speaking to elders or in formal settings.
--- word ---
term: 기억에서
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 기억 + -에서
meaning: from memory
notes: The particle '-에서' (eseo) indicates the starting point or origin of an action, meaning 'from' a place. While it often marks a location where an action happens, here it means 'from out of my memory'.
--- word ---
term: 지우려고
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 지우다 + -(으)려고
meaning: in order to erase, trying to erase
notes: This is part of the '-(으)려고 하다' (-(eu)ryeogo hada) grammar pattern, which expresses intention ('intend to', 'plan to'). The final '하다' (hada) is often omitted in casual speech, as seen here.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)려고 (하다)
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or plan to do something. It translates to 'in order to', 'planning to', or 'going to'.
example: 한국에 가려고 돈을 모으고 있어요. (I'm saving money to go to Korea.)
level: Beginner

meaning: Hmm.

meaning: Hmm, umm

=== [36:18] 그러게 되게 많이 먹은 거 같은데 ===
meaning: You're right, it seems like I ate a lot.
tone: Informal, agreeing, reflective.
--- word ---
term: 그러게
type: Interjection/Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: I know, right?; Indeed
notes: Used to express agreement with what someone has just said. It carries a nuance of 'I was thinking the same thing'. It's very common in casual conversation.
--- word ---
term: 되게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: very, really
notes: A colloquial adverb meaning 'very' or 'extremely'. It's often used in spoken Korean instead of more standard adverbs like '아주' (aju) or '매우' (maeu). '정말' (jeongmal) and '진짜' (jinjja) are also common alternatives.
--- word ---
term: 많이
type: Adverb
breakdown: 많다 + -이
meaning: a lot, much
notes: The adverbial form of the adjective '많다' (manta), meaning 'to be many/much'. It modifies a verb to indicate that the action was done to a great extent.
--- word ---
term: 먹은
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 먹다 + -은
meaning: (that I) ate
notes: This is the past tense adjective form of the verb '먹다' (meokda, to eat). The '-은' (eun) ending turns the verb into a modifier that can describe a following noun, in this case, '거' (geo).
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Bound Noun
breakdown: 것 (contraction)
meaning: thing, fact
notes: '거' (geo) is a very common spoken contraction of '것' (geot), which means 'thing' or 'fact'. Here, '먹은 거' (meogeun geo) means 'the thing that I ate' or 'the fact that I ate'.
--- word ---
term: 같은데
type: Adjective + Ending
breakdown: 같다 + -은데
meaning: it seems like...
notes: The '-ㄴ/은데' (-n/eunde) ending is used to provide background information or context before making a statement, asking a question, or trailing off. Here, it softens the statement, making it sound less direct.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -(으)ㄴ/는 거 같다
meaning: Expresses a guess, opinion, or impression. It translates to 'it seems like', 'I think that', or 'it looks like'.
example: 비가 오는 거 같아요. (I think it's raining.)
level: Beginner

meaning: A connective ending used to link two clauses, actions, or states in sequence or to simply list them. It translates to 'and' or 'and then'.

--- word ---
term: 더
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: more
notes: Used before an adjective or adverb to form a comparative, meaning 'more ___'.

=== [36:44] 묽은 느낌이긴 하다 ===
meaning: it does have a watery feel.
tone: Informal, descriptive, confirming with slight reservation.
--- word ---
term: 묽은
type: Adjective (modifier form)
breakdown: 묽다 + -은
meaning: watery, thin
notes: The adjective '묽다' (mukda) means 'to be thin' or 'watery' (in consistency). The '-은' (eun) ending changes it into a form that can modify a noun, in this case '느낌' (neukkim).
--- word ---
term: 느낌이긴
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 느낌 + -이기는
meaning: it is a feeling that...
notes: '느낌' (neukkim) means 'feeling' or 'impression'. '-이긴' (igin) is a contraction of '-이기는' (igineun), which is part of the '-긴 하다' grammar pattern used to acknowledge a point.
--- word ---
term: 하다
type: Verb
breakdown: 
meaning: to be, to do
notes: Here, '하다' (hada) combines with the preceding noun and particle to complete the '-긴 하다' pattern. '느낌이긴 하다' means 'it is indeed a feeling (of being watery)'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -긴 하다
meaning: Used to acknowledge or agree with a fact, but often with a slight reservation or a contrasting thought to follow. It can be translated as 'it is true that... but...', 'it does...', or 'indeed...'.
example: 비싸긴 한데 맛있어요. (It is indeed expensive, but it's delicious.)
level: Intermediate

=== [36:46] 다르다면 그런 거 ===
meaning: If it's different, it's something like that.
tone: Informal, trailing off, clarifying a previous point.
--- word ---
term: 다르다면
type: verb
breakdown: 다르다 + -(ㄴ)다면
meaning: if it's different
notes: 다르다 is an irregular verb; the '르' drops and an extra 'ㄹ' is added before endings like -아/어요, making it 달라요 (dallayo). -(ㄴ)다면 is a conditional ending used for hypothetical or less likely situations, similar to 'if' in English. It's slightly more formal or literary than -(으)면.
--- word ---
term: 그런
type: adjective
breakdown: 그렇다 + -(으)ㄴ
meaning: like that, that kind of
notes: This is the adjective form of 그렇다 (geureota, 'to be so/like that'), used to modify a noun. It's part of a set: 이런 (ireon, 'like this'), 그런 (geureon, 'like that'), 저런 (jeoreon, 'like that over there').
--- word ---
term: 거
type: noun
breakdown: 것 (contraction)
meaning: thing, stuff
notes: 거 (geo) is the colloquial, spoken contraction of the noun 것 (geot), meaning 'thing'. It's extremely common in everyday conversation but less so in formal writing. For example, '이거' (igeo) for '이것' (igeot, 'this thing').
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(ㄴ)다면
meaning: A conditional ending expressing a hypothetical situation ('if...'). It often carries a nuance of being less probable than the more common -(으)면 ending.
example: 돈이 많다면 세계 여행을 할 거예요. (If I had a lot of money, I would travel the world.)
level: Intermediate

=== [37:09] 그리고 ===
meaning: And...
tone: Transitional, connecting to a new topic.
--- word ---
term: 그리고
type: conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: and, and then
notes: 그리고 (geurigo) is a fundamental conjunction used to connect sentences or clauses. While it means 'and', it often implies a sequence of events, meaning 'and then'. For connecting nouns, the particle -하고 (hago) or -(와/과) (wa/gwa) is used instead (e.g., 빵하고 우유 - bread and milk).

=== [37:10] 선물로 이걸 샀어 ===
meaning: I bought this as a gift.

--- word ---
term: 선물로
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 선물 + -(으)로
meaning: as a gift
notes: The particle -(으)로 has many meanings, including 'with/by' (for tools), 'to/towards' (for direction), and here, 'as' (indicating a role or purpose). Since 선물 (seonmul) ends in a consonant (ㄹ), 로 is used. If the noun ended in a vowel, 으로 would be used.
--- word ---
term: 이걸
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 이것 + 을 (contraction)
meaning: this thing (object)
notes: 이걸 (igeol) is the spoken contraction of 이것을 (igeoseul). 이것 means 'this thing' and -을 is the object particle. In casual speech, contractions like 이걸, 그걸 (geugeol, 'that thing'), and 저걸 (jeogeol, 'that thing over there') are very common.
--- word ---
term: 샀어
type: verb
breakdown: 사다 + -았어
meaning: bought
notes: This is the past tense, informal, non-polite (반말, banmal) form of the verb 사다 (sada, 'to buy'). The speaker, Hyejin, uses banmal consistently with her fans (Cheong-i), creating a friendly, intimate tone. The polite equivalent would be 샀어요 (sass-eoyo).

=== [37:15] 너무 귀엽지 ===

tone: Informal, enthusiastic, seeking agreement.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, very
notes: Traditionally, 너무 (neomu) meant 'too much' in a negative sense. However, in modern colloquial Korean, it's widely used to mean 'very' or 'so' in a positive sense, like in this sentence. This is a very common feature of casual speech.

meaning: it's cute, right?
notes: The ending -지 (ji) is used to ask a question to which the speaker assumes the listener will agree. It creates a sense of shared knowledge or opinion. meaning: A sentence ending used to confirm something the speaker already knows or assumes the listener will agree with. It softens the statement and invites confirmation.
example: 오늘 날씨 좋지? (The weather's nice today, right?)

=== [37:19] 이게 뭔 줄 알아? ===
meaning: Do you know what this is?
tone: Informal, engaging, quizzing the audience.
--- word ---
term: 이게
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 이것 + 이 (contraction)
meaning: this thing (subject)
notes: 이게 (ige) is the spoken contraction of 이것이 (igeos-i). 이것 means 'this thing' and -이 is the subject particle. Using contractions like 이게 and 이걸 is a key feature of natural, fluent Korean speech.
--- word ---
term: 뭔
type: pronoun
breakdown: 무엇인 (contraction)
meaning: what
notes: In this context, 뭔 (mwon) is a contraction of 무엇인 (mueos-in), which is 'what' + the verb 'to be' (이다) in a modifier form. It's part of the larger grammar pattern in this sentence.
--- word ---
term: 줄
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: way, method; (in this pattern) the fact that
notes: The noun 줄 (jul) has several meanings, but in the pattern '-(으)ㄴ/는 줄 알다/모르다', it functions as a placeholder for a fact or piece of information. It doesn't translate directly but is essential to the grammar pattern.
--- word ---
term: 알아?
type: verb
breakdown: 알다 + -아?
meaning: do you know?
notes: The informal, non-polite (반말, banmal) question form of 알다 (alda, 'to know'). The standard polite form would be 알아요? (arayo?), and the formal version is 아십니까? (asimnikka?).
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄴ/는 줄 알다/모르다
meaning: Used to express knowing or not knowing a fact, or how to do something. '...what/that/how... I know/don't know'.
example: 김치가 이렇게 매운 줄 몰랐어요. (I didn't know kimchi was this spicy.)
level: Intermediate

=== [37:27] 이거 먹은 거 가지고 올까? ===
meaning: Should I bring the thing I ate?
tone: Informal, suggesting an action, asking for audience opinion.
--- word ---
term: 이거
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 이것 (contraction)
meaning: this thing
notes: A common colloquial contraction of 이것 (igeot). Used frequently in spoken Korean instead of the full form.
--- word ---
term: 먹은
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 먹다 + ~(으)ㄴ
meaning: that (I) ate
notes: The ~(으)ㄴ (eun/n) ending attaches to a verb stem to turn it into a past-tense adjective that can modify a noun. For example, 먹은 음식 (meogeun eumsik) means 'the food that I ate'.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Noun (dependent)
breakdown: 것 (contraction)
meaning: thing
notes: A colloquial contraction of the dependent noun 것 (geot), which means 'thing' or 'fact'. It almost always needs a modifier before it, like in '먹은 거' (the thing I ate).
--- word ---
term: 가지고
type: Verb (connective form)
breakdown: 가지다 + ~고
meaning: holding and...
notes: The verb 가지다 (gajida) means 'to have' or 'to hold'. The ~고 (go) ending connects it to the next verb. The phrase '가지고 오다' (gajigo oda) literally means 'to hold and come', which translates to 'to bring'.
--- word ---
term: 올까?
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + ~(으)ㄹ까?
meaning: shall I come/bring?
notes: The ~(으)ㄹ까? (eulkka?) ending is used to make a suggestion or ask for the listener's opinion about a future action. It's in the informal 반말 (banmal) style. The polite version would be 올까요? (olkkayo?).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~(으)ㄴ 것/거
meaning: Turns a past-tense action into a noun phrase, meaning 'the thing that one did'.
example: 어제 본 영화는 재미있었어요. (The movie I saw yesterday was fun.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~(으)ㄹ까(요)?
meaning: Used to make a suggestion ('Shall we...?') or ask for an opinion ('Do you think...?').
example: 우리 저녁 뭐 먹을까? (What shall we eat for dinner?)
level: Beginner

=== [37:39] 자 봐봐 ===
meaning: Okay, look.
tone: Informal, attention-grabbing, transitional.
--- word ---
term: 자
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Okay / Now / Well
notes: A very common interjection used to get someone's attention, transition to a new topic, or start an action. Similar to saying 'Alright, then...' in English.
--- word ---
term: 봐봐
type: Verb
breakdown: 보다 + ~아/어 봐
meaning: take a look
notes: A colloquial and softened command form of 보다 (boda), 'to see/look'. It's a repetition of 봐 (bwa), the basic informal command. 봐봐 (bwabwa) sounds friendlier and more suggestive than a simple '봐!'. It's like saying 'Have a look' or 'Check it out'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 보다
meaning: Indicates trying or attempting an action. When used as a command like '봐봐', it softens the request, making it a suggestion to 'try looking'.
example: 이거 한번 먹어 봐. (Try eating this.)
level: Beginner

=== [37:41] 뭐예요 ===
meaning: What is it?
tone: Informal polite, questioning.
--- word ---
term: 뭐예요
type: Pronoun + Copula
breakdown: 뭐 + ~예요
meaning: What is it?
notes: 뭐 (mwo) is a contraction of 무엇 (mueot), 'what'. ~예요 (yeyo) is the polite ending for 'is/am/are' that attaches to nouns ending in a vowel. It's interesting that she uses this polite form when she usually speaks in informal 반말 (banmal). This kind of code-switching is common and can be used to be playful or to quote a question.

=== [37:43] 선물용이니까 내가 먼저 ===
meaning: Since it's for a gift, I [shouldn't be the] first [to touch it].
tone: Informal, explanatory, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 선물용이니까
type: noun + copula + conjunction
breakdown: 선물용 (for gift use) + -이다 (to be) + -니까 (because)
meaning: because it's for a gift
notes: 선물용 is a compound of 선물 (gift) + 용 (for use). The -(이)니까 ending indicates a reason that the speaker assumes is known or obvious. It's a common way to provide justification in casual conversation. The formal equivalent is 선물용이니까요.
--- word ---
term: 내가
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 (I) + -가 (subject particle)
meaning: I
notes: 내가 is the informal way to say 'I' as the subject of a sentence. In many modern Seoul dialects, its pronunciation is identical to 네가 (you), so speakers often use 니가 for 'you' to avoid confusion. The formal equivalent is 제가.
--- word ---
term: 먼저
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: first
notes: 먼저 means 'first' in terms of order or priority (e.g., 'You go first'). It is different from 처음, which means 'the first time'. For example: 먼저 하세요 (Please do it first) vs. 한국에 처음 왔어요 (I came to Korea for the first time).
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + -(이)니까
meaning: Indicates the preceding noun is the reason or cause for the following clause. Translates to 'because it is (noun)...' or 'since it is (noun)...'.
example: 학생이니까 돈이 없어요. (Because I'm a student, I don't have money.)
level: Beginner

=== [37:56] 쪼물르면 안 돼 ===
meaning: You can't squish it.
tone: Informal, self-admonishing.
--- word ---
term: 쪼물르면
type: verb
breakdown: 쪼물르다 (to squish/knead) + -면 (if)
meaning: if you squish
notes: 쪼물르다 is a colloquial and somewhat onomatopoeic verb for 'to knead' or 'squish playfully'. The standard verb is 주무르다. The -(으)면 ending creates a conditional clause ('if/when').
--- word ---
term: 안
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: 안 is a short-form negator placed directly before a verb or adjective. It is a contraction of 아니 and is very common in spoken Korean. The long-form negation is V + -지 않다 (e.g., 쪼물르지 않다).
--- word ---
term: 돼
type: verb
breakdown: 되다 (to be okay/possible) + -어 (informal ending)
meaning: it's okay
notes: 돼 is a contraction of 되어. The phrase '-(으)면 안 되다' means 'one must not' or 'it is not allowed'. Learners often confuse the spelling of 돼 and 되. A simple trick: if the word '해' (from 하다) fits grammatically, use 돼. If '하' fits, use 되.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)면 안 되다
meaning: Expresses prohibition or that something is not allowed. It translates to 'you shouldn't...', 'you must not...', or 'it's not okay if...'.
example: 수업 시간에 자면 안 돼요. (You shouldn't sleep during class.)
level: Beginner

=== [37:58] 그건 예의에 어긋나 ===
meaning: That's against good manners.
tone: Informal, stating a principle.
--- word ---
term: 그건
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 그것 (that thing) + -은 (topic particle)
meaning: That
notes: 그건 is a common contraction of 그것은. 그것 refers to something previously mentioned or near the listener. The topic particle -은/는 marks what the sentence is about.
--- word ---
term: 예의에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 예의 (manners) + -에 (particle)
meaning: to/against manners
notes: 예의 means 'etiquette' or 'manners,' a significant concept in Korean culture. The particle -에 here indicates the standard or rule that is being violated.
--- word ---
term: 어긋나
type: verb
breakdown: 어긋나다 (to go against) + -아/어 (informal ending)
meaning: goes against
notes: 어긋나다 literally means 'to be misaligned'. Metaphorically, it means to go against rules, expectations, or norms. This is the informal, present tense conjugation used in 반말 (casual speech).

=== [38:00] 그렇다 ===
meaning: That's right.
tone: Informal, declarative, as if talking to oneself.
--- word ---
term: 그렇다
type: adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: to be so / to be like that
notes: This is the dictionary form (plain form) of the adjective. Using the plain form to end a sentence gives it a narrative or declarative feel, common in writing or when making an exclamation to oneself. The casual spoken equivalent is 그래.

=== [38:04] 또 떼야 돼 ===
meaning: I have to take it off again.
tone: Informal, matter-of-fact, slightly resigned.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: again
notes: 또 implies the repetition of an action. It can be distinguished from 다시, which also means 'again' but often carries the nuance of 'starting over' or 'doing one more time'.
--- word ---
term: 떼야
type: verb
breakdown: 떼다 (to detach) + -어야 하다 (must do)
meaning: have to take off
notes: 떼야 is a spoken contraction of 떼어야. It is part of the grammar pattern V + -어야/아야 되다/하다, which expresses obligation or necessity.
--- word ---
term: 돼
type: verb
breakdown: 되다 (to become)
meaning: have to
notes: 돼 is a contraction of 되어 and completes the -어야/아야 되다 pattern. Using 되다 is slightly softer than using 하다, often implying a necessity arising from circumstances rather than a strong personal will.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -어야/아야 되다
meaning: Expresses obligation or necessity, equivalent to 'must' or 'have to' in English.
example: 내일 일찍 일어나야 돼요. (I have to wake up early tomorrow.)
level: Beginner

=== [38:06] 흰수염 ===
meaning: Whitebeard.
tone: Informal, observational.
--- word ---
term: 흰수염
type: noun
breakdown: 희다 (to be white) + -ㄴ (modifier) + 수염 (beard)
meaning: white beard
notes: 흰 is the noun-modifying form of the adjective 희다 (to be white). 수염 can refer to a beard, mustache, or whiskers. '흰수염' is the Korean name for the character 'Whitebeard' from the popular manga/anime *One Piece*.

=== [38:15] 흰수염 고래다 ===
meaning: It's a whitebeard whale.
tone: Informal, declarative, a moment of realization.
--- word ---
term: 흰수염
type: noun
breakdown: 희다 (to be white) + -ㄴ (modifier) + 수염 (beard)
meaning: white beard
notes: 흰 is the noun-modifying form of the adjective 희다 (to be white). The actual Korean term for 'blue whale' is 흰긴수염고래 (white long-bearded whale), so she may be simplifying it or making a joke.
--- word ---
term: 고래다
type: noun + copula
breakdown: 고래 (whale) + -이다 (to be)
meaning: it is a whale
notes: The -다 ending is the plain form of the copula -이다. Using it here creates a declarative or exclamatory statement, as if making a discovery. The casual spoken equivalent would be 고래야.

=== [38:18] 흰수염 고래다 ===
meaning: It's a whitebeard whale.
tone: Informal, repetitive, emphatic.
--- word ---
term: 흰수염
type: noun
breakdown: 희다 (to be white) + -ㄴ (modifier) + 수염 (beard)
meaning: white beard
notes: 흰 is the noun-modifying form of the adjective 희다 (to be white). When an adjective stem ends in 'ㅎ' like in 하얗다 (to be white), the 'ㅎ' is often dropped and -ㄴ is added to modify a noun (e.g., 하얀 집 - white house).
--- word ---
term: 고래다
type: noun + copula
breakdown: 고래 (whale) + -이다 (to be)
meaning: it is a whale
notes: Repeating the phrase with the declarative -다 ending adds emphasis to her discovery or statement. This narrative-style ending is common in monologues or when talking to oneself.

=== [38:21] 어떻게 생각해? ===
meaning: What do you think?
tone: Informal, direct question.
--- word ---
term: 어떻게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: how
notes: Often used to ask 'how' something is done or 'what' one thinks. It can be confused with '어때' (how is it?), which asks for an opinion on the state of something. '어떻게 생각해?' (What do you think?) asks for an opinion on a matter, while '이거 어때?' (How is this?) asks for an opinion on an object.
--- word ---
term: 생각해?
type: Verb
breakdown: 생각하다 + -아/어?
meaning: do you think?
notes: This is the informal, non-polite present tense form of 생각하다 (to think). The -요 ending is dropped for 반말 (banmal), casual speech used with close friends, which the speaker uses with her fans. The polite form would be '생각해요?'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 어떻게 생각해(요)?
meaning: A common set phrase used to ask for someone's opinion or thoughts on a specific topic. Literally 'How do you think?'.
example: 이 디자인 어떻게 생각해요? (What do you think of this design?)
level: Beginner

=== [38:22] 흰수염 고래 ===
meaning: A blue whale.
tone: Noun phrase, declarative.
--- word ---
term: 흰수염
type: Noun
breakdown: 희다 + -ㄴ + 수염
meaning: white beard
notes: Literally 'white beard'. This is a compound noun. The adjective 희다 (to be white) modifies the noun 수염 (beard). The -ㄴ is an adnominal ending that turns the adjective into a modifier. 흰수염고래 is the Korean name for a blue whale, referencing the white baleen plates in its mouth that can look like a beard.
--- word ---
term: 고래
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: whale
notes: A standalone noun for 'whale'. Combined with 흰수염, it forms the specific name for 'blue whale'.

=== [38:27] 산타 할아버지 ===

breakdown: 
meaning: Santa
notes: A loanword from English, 'Santa'.
--- word ---
term: 할아버지
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: grandfather
notes: Means 'grandfather'. It's also used as a respectful term to address any elderly man. Combining '산타' and '할아버지' is the standard way to say 'Santa Claus' in Korean, literally 'Santa Grandfather'.

=== [38:35] 올해 일찍 오셨대 ===
meaning: They say he came early this year.
tone: Informal, reporting hearsay.
--- word ---
term: 올해
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: this year
notes: A common noun for 'this year'. Compare with 작년 (last year) and 내년 (next year).
--- word ---
term: 일찍
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: early
notes: An adverb meaning 'early'. Its opposite is 늦게 (late).
--- word ---
term: 오셨대
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + -시- + -었- + -대
meaning: they say (he) came
notes: A very condensed form. It breaks down into: 오다 (to come) + -시- (honorific suffix, showing respect for Santa) + -었- (past tense) + -대 (a contraction of -다고 해, meaning 'they say' or 'I heard'). This form is common in casual speech to report something you've heard from others.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -대(요)
meaning: A colloquial ending used to quote or report something one has heard. It's a contraction of -다고 해(요) for verbs or -다고 해(요) for adjectives.
example: 그 식당이 아주 맛있대. (I heard that restaurant is very delicious.)
level: Intermediate

=== [38:38] 그러니까 ===
meaning: I know, right?
tone: Informal, emphatic agreement.
--- word ---
term: 그러니까
type: Conjunction / Adverb
breakdown: 그렇다 + -(으)니까
meaning: Exactly / I know
notes: Textbook meaning is 'therefore' or 'so'. However, in conversation, it's very frequently used as a standalone expression to mean 'I know, right?', 'Exactly!', or 'That's what I'm saying!'. It shows strong agreement with what the other person just said.

=== [38:41] 그러니까 ===
meaning: I know, right?
tone: Informal, emphatic agreement, repetitive.
--- word ---
term: 그러니까
type: Conjunction / Adverb
breakdown: 그렇다 + -(으)니까
meaning: Exactly / I know
notes: The speaker repeats the word for emphasis. Repetition is a common feature in spoken Korean to add stress or show strong feelings. As in the previous segment, this is used colloquially to mean 'That's what I'm saying!'.

=== [38:45] 이게 무슨 일이야 ===
meaning: What is going on?
tone: Informal, rhetorical question expressing surprise.
--- word ---
term: 이게
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 이것 + 이
meaning: this (thing)
notes: A common contraction of 이것 (this thing) and the subject particle 이. In spoken Korean, contractions like 이게 (이것이), 그게 (그것이), and 저게 (저것이) are used far more often than their full forms.
--- word ---
term: 무슨
type: Determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: what kind of
notes: An interrogative determiner that must be followed by a noun. It asks about the nature or type of something. It's different from 뭐 (what), which can stand alone as a pronoun. For example, '무슨 일이야?' (What kind of situation is this?) vs. '이게 뭐야?' (What is this?).
--- word ---
term: 일이야
type: Noun + Verb
breakdown: 일 + 이다 + -야
meaning: is the matter
notes: 일 means 'matter', 'affair', or 'work'. It's combined with the informal ending -이야, which is the casual form of -이에요 used after a noun ending in a consonant. The full phrase '이게 무슨 일이야?' is a very common expression of surprise or confusion, like 'What's happening?' or 'What on earth is this?'.

=== [38:47] 징 ===
meaning: Jing.
tone: Onomatopoeic, playful.
--- word ---
term: 징
type: Onomatopoeia
breakdown: 
meaning: jing / jingle
notes: An onomatopoeic word (의성어) representing a ringing or jingling sound, like a bell or sleigh bell. Korean uses a rich vocabulary of onomatopoeic and mimetic words to make descriptions more vivid.

breakdown: 
meaning: Hi / Hello
notes: A common informal greeting (반말, banmal). It can mean both 'hello' and 'goodbye'. It's used with friends, family, or people younger than you. The formal equivalent is 안녕하세요 (annyeonghaseyo).

=== [38:58] 라이브 방금 이제 켠 거 같다 또 ===

tone: Informal, observational, self-aware.
--- word ---
term: 라이브
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Live stream
notes: A Konglish (Korean-style English) word for a 'live broadcast'. It's extremely common in the context of K-pop idols and online streamers.
--- word ---
term: 방금
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Just now
notes: Refers to a very recent past, usually seconds or minutes ago. Often confused by learners with 금방 (geumbang), which refers to the immediate future ('in a moment, soon').
--- word ---
term: 이제
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Now
notes: Indicates a change of state or the start of a new action from this point forward. It's different from 지금 (jigeum), which means 'right now' at this exact moment. Here, it emphasizes the transition to the 'on' state.
--- word ---
term: 켠
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 켜다 + -ㄴ
meaning: (that was) turned on
notes: The past tense adjectival form of the verb 켜다 (kyeoda), meaning 'to turn on'. It modifies the following noun, 거 (geo).
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Noun (dependent)
breakdown: 것
meaning: Thing / the act of
notes: 거 (geo) is the colloquial contraction of 것 (geot). In this context, 켠 거 means 'the act of turning it on' or 'the fact that it was turned on'.
--- word ---
term: 같다
type: Adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: to seem / to be like
notes: Used here in its base form, common in casual speech (반말, banmal). It's part of the '-(으)ㄴ 것 같다' pattern, expressing a guess or opinion.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Again
notes: Indicates repetition. Its placement at the end of the sentence is a feature of casual speech, used for emphasis or as an afterthought.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄴ 것 같다
meaning: Expresses a guess or supposition about a past action. 'It seems/looks like (someone) did...'
example: 친구가 벌써 집에 간 것 같아요. (It seems my friend has already gone home.)
level: Beginner

=== [39:03] 그러니까 ===

breakdown: 그렇다 + -(으)니까

=== [39:04] 정말 이거는 정말 맛있었어 ===

breakdown:

breakdown: 이것 + 는
meaning: This one (as for this)

term: 정말
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Really / Truly
notes: Repeated for extra emphasis to express how delicious the snack was. This kind of repetition is very common in spoken Korean to convey strong feelings.

breakdown: 맛있다 + -었어
meaning: was delicious

=== [39:09] 정말 맛있었고 ===
meaning: It was really delicious, and...
tone: Informal, trailing, connecting thoughts.
--- word ---
term: 정말
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Really / Truly
notes: An adverb used for emphasis. Synonyms include 진짜 (jinjja) and 참 (cham).
--- word ---
term: 맛있었고
type: Adjective (conjugated)
breakdown: 맛있다 + -었- + -고
meaning: was delicious and...
notes: The -고 (-go) ending connects two clauses. It can mean 'and' or 'and then'. Here, it links the fact that the snack was delicious to another thought she is about to share, leaving the sentence hanging.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-고
meaning: A connective ending used to link two clauses. It can indicate a sequence of events ('and then') or simply list two related facts ('and').
example: 어제는 비가 오고 바람이 불었어요. (Yesterday it rained and the wind blew.)
level: Beginner

=== [39:14] 저만 ===
meaning: Just me...
tone: Informal, hesitant, introductory.
--- word ---
term: 저만
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 저 + -만
meaning: Only me / Just me
notes: 저 (jeo) is the humble first-person pronoun 'I'. The particle -만 (man) means 'only' or 'just'. Even though she is speaking in informal 반말 (banmal), she uses the humble 저 (jeo) instead of the plain 나 (na), which can add a slightly more polite or careful nuance, or can just be a personal habit.

=== [39:19] 되게 뭔가를 많이 샀거든 ===
meaning: You see, I bought a lot of stuff.
tone: Informal, explanatory, providing background.
--- word ---
term: 되게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Very / Really
notes: A colloquial adverb for emphasis, similar to 정말 (jeongmal) or 아주 (aju). It's very common in casual conversation, especially among younger speakers.
--- word ---
term: 뭔가를
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 뭔가 + -를
meaning: Something
notes: 뭔가 (mwonga) is a contraction of 무엇인가 (mueosinga) and means 'something'. Here, it's used vaguely to mean 'some stuff' or 'various things'. -를 (reul) is the object particle.
--- word ---
term: 많이
type: Adverb
breakdown: 많다 + -이
meaning: A lot / Many
notes: The adverbial form of the adjective 많다 (manta), 'to be many/much'. It modifies the verb 샀다 (satda) to indicate the quantity bought.
--- word ---
term: 샀거든
type: Verb (conjugated)
breakdown: 사다 + -았- + -거든
meaning: I bought, you see
notes: The base verb is 사다 (sada), 'to buy'. The ending -거든 (geodeun) is used to provide a reason or background information for a statement. It creates a nuance of '...you see,' or 'because...'. It's used in informal speech.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-(으)ㄴ/거든(요)
meaning: Used to provide a reason or background information that the listener might not be aware of. It has a nuance of '...you see,' or '...because...'. It's common in spoken Korean.
example: 먼저 갈게. 약속이 있거든. (I'll go first. I have an appointment, you see.)
level: Intermediate

=== [39:26] 재미 삼아 이런 걸 한번 사봤어 ===
meaning: I just bought something like this for fun.
tone: Informal, explanatory, casual.
--- word ---
term: 재미
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Fun
notes: A common noun for 'fun' or 'interest'. It's the root of the adjective 재미있다 (jaemiitda), 'to be fun/interesting'.
--- word ---
term: 삼아
type: Postpositional verb
breakdown: 삼다 + -아
meaning: For the sake of / As
notes: From the verb 삼다 (samda), 'to make something into'. The pattern 'Noun 삼아' means 'for the purpose of Noun' or 'as Noun'. So, 재미 삼아 means 'for fun' or 'just for kicks'.
--- word ---
term: 이런
type: Determiner
breakdown: 이렇다 + -ㄴ
meaning: This kind of
notes: The modifier form of the adjective 이렇다 (ireota), 'to be like this'. It modifies the following noun, 걸 (geol).
--- word ---
term: 걸
type: Pronoun + Particle (contracted)
breakdown: 것 + 을
meaning: thing (object)
notes: A common contraction of 것을 (geoseul), which is the dependent noun 것 (geot) 'thing' plus the object particle 을 (eul). It's frequently used in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 한번
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Once / (to try)
notes: Literally means 'one time'. When used with the V-아/어 보다 grammar pattern, it means 'to try doing something once' and softens the statement, making it sound more casual.
--- word ---
term: 사봤어
type: Verb (conjugated)
breakdown: 사다 + -아 보다 + -았어
meaning: tried buying
notes: This combines the verb 사다 (sada), 'to buy', with the grammar pattern -아/어 보다 (-a/eo boda), which means 'to try doing something'. The final -았어 (-asseo) makes it past tense and informal (반말, banmal).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 보다
meaning: Indicates trying or experiencing an action. It's often translated as 'to try doing V'. It gives a sense of testing something out or doing it to see what happens.
example: 김치를 먹어 봤어요? (Have you ever tried eating kimchi?)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun 삼아
meaning: Indicates the purpose or reason for an action, meaning 'for the sake of Noun' or 'as Noun'.
example: 운동 삼아 걸어왔어요. (I walked for the sake of exercise.)
level: Intermediate

=== [39:35] 손톱에 붙이는 거 ===
meaning: The thing you stick on your fingernails.
tone: Informal, descriptive.
--- word ---
term: 손톱에
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 손톱 (fingernail) + -에 (on/at)
meaning: on the fingernail
notes: The particle -에 indicates a static location. It marks 'fingernail' as the place where the action of 'sticking' happens. It can also be used for time, destinations, and more.
--- word ---
term: 붙이는
type: Verb (modifying form)
breakdown: 붙이다 (to stick/attach) + -는
meaning: that you stick / for sticking
notes: The -는 ending transforms a verb into a present-tense adjective that modifies a noun. It describes an ongoing action or a general characteristic. For example, 먹는 음식 (food that one eats).
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Noun (dependent)
breakdown: 것 (thing) contracted to 거
meaning: thing, stuff
notes: 거 is the colloquial, spoken contraction of the dependent noun 것 (thing, stuff, the one). It is extremely common in informal speech. The full, more formal phrase would be 붙이는 것.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb + -는 것/거
meaning: A common way to nominalize a verb phrase, turning it into a noun clause. It can mean 'the act of V-ing', 'the thing that one V-s', or 'the fact that...'.
example: 제가 좋아하는 것은 음악이에요. (The thing I like is music.)
level: Beginner

=== [39:38] 지금은 안 붙일 거고 ===
meaning: I'm not going to put them on now, and...
tone: Informal, explanatory, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 지금은
type: Adverb + Particle
breakdown: 지금 (now) + -은 (topic/contrast particle)
meaning: as for now
notes: The particle -은 adds a sense of contrast, implying 'I won't do it *now*, but I might later.' It distinguishes 'now' from other potential times.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: 안 is a short-form negator placed directly before a verb or adjective. It's very common in spoken Korean. The long-form negation is -지 않다 (e.g., 붙이지 않을 거고).
--- word ---
term: 붙일
type: Verb (modifying form)
breakdown: 붙이다 (to stick/attach) + -ㄹ (future tense modifier)
meaning: will stick
notes: The -(으)ㄹ ending modifies a following noun to indicate a future action or intention. Here, it modifies the implied noun 거 (thing).
--- word ---
term: 거고
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 것 (thing) contracted to 거 + -이고 (and) contracted to -고
meaning: ...and
notes: This is a contraction of 것이고. The pattern V-(으)ㄹ 거고 is a very common colloquial way to state a future plan and then connect it to the next clause with 'and'.

=== [39:39] 나중에 뭐 붙일 일이 있으면 ===
meaning: Later, if there's an occasion to put them on...
tone: Informal, hypothetical, continuing a thought.
--- word ---
term: 나중에
type: Adverb
breakdown: 나중 (later) + -에 (at/in)
meaning: later
notes: 나중에 refers to a future point in time. It's often interchangeable with 이따가, but 나중에 can imply a more distant future (e.g., days, weeks), while 이따가 usually means 'a little later today'.
--- word ---
term: 뭐
type: Pronoun/Adverb
breakdown: 무엇 (what) contracted to 뭐
meaning: like, something
notes: Here, 뭐 is not a question word ('what'). It's used as a filler word to make the speech sound more natural and less direct, similar to 'like' or 'you know' in English.
--- word ---
term: 붙일
type: Verb (modifying form)
breakdown: 붙이다 (to stick/attach) + -ㄹ (future tense modifier)
meaning: to put on / that I will put on
notes: The future modifying form -(으)ㄹ turns the verb into an adjective describing the following noun, 일 (occasion).
--- word ---
term: 일이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 일 (matter, occasion, work) + -이 (subject particle)
meaning: an occasion
notes: The noun 일 has many meanings. In this context, it means 'occasion' or 'reason'. The particle -이 marks it as the subject of the verb 있다 (to exist).
--- word ---
term: 있으면
type: Verb (conditional)
breakdown: 있다 (to exist, have) + -(으)면 (if/when)
meaning: if there is
notes: The -(으)면 ending attaches to verb and adjective stems to create a conditional clause, meaning 'if' or 'when'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄹ 일이 있다/없다
meaning: A set phrase meaning 'to have an occasion/reason to V' or 'to have no occasion/reason to V'.
example: 오늘은 친구를 만날 일이 있어요. (I have an occasion to meet a friend today.)
level: Intermediate

--- word ---
term: 배고파서

breakdown: 배고프다 (to be hungry) + -아서 (because, so)
meaning: because I was hungry
notes: The -아서/어서 ending connects two clauses, indicating the first is the reason for the second. It's often used for natural consequences or emotional states.
--- word ---

breakdown: 자다 (to sleep) + -ㅁ (nominalizing suffix)
meaning: sleep

meaning: it seems, I guess
notes: The pattern V/A + -나 보다 or -(으)ㄴ가 보다 is used to express a guess or assumption based on some evidence. Here, the evidence is that she's suddenly sleepy after eating.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -나 보다 / -(으)ㄴ가 보다
meaning: Expresses a guess or assumption, meaning 'it seems that...' or 'I guess that...'. It's based on some observed fact or situation.
example: 저 사람 기분이 안 좋은가 봐요. (It looks like that person is in a bad mood.)

=== [40:04] 아직까지도 ===
meaning: Still, even now...
tone: Informal, emphatic, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 아직까지도
type: adverb
breakdown: 아직 + -까지 + -도
meaning: still, even now
notes: A combination of three parts: 아직 (still), -까지 (until/up to), and -도 (even/also). Together, they create a very emphatic way of saying 'even up to this very moment'. It carries more weight than just 아직.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun/Adverb + 까지도
meaning: Emphasizes 'even up to [that point]' or 'even still'. It adds the nuance of 'even' (-도) to the limit set by -까지.
example: 어제까지도 몰랐어요. (I didn't know even up until yesterday.)
level: Intermediate

=== [40:06] 어떡해 ===
meaning: What should I do?
tone: Informal, expressive, feigning distress.
--- word ---
term: 어떡해
type: expression
breakdown: 어떻게 해
meaning: What should I do?
notes: A very common contraction of 어떻게 해 (eotteoke hae). It's an exclamation used in situations of worry, frustration, or being overwhelmed. A common mistake for learners is confusing it with the adverb 어떻게 (eotteoke), which means 'how'. 어떡해 is a full predicate and can end a sentence, while 어떻게 cannot.

term: 붙는

=== [40:40] 뭔지 알지 ===

=== [40:48] 이 안개를 뚫고 ===

--- word ---
term: 이
type: Determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: this
notes: Used before a noun to specify something close to the speaker. It corresponds to 'this' or 'these'. Compare with '그' (that, near the listener) and '저' (that, far from both).

meaning: fog (as an object)
notes: 안개 means 'fog'. The particle '를' marks it as the direct object of the verb. '를' is used after nouns ending in a vowel, while '을' is used after nouns ending in a consonant (e.g., '책을' - a book).

meaning: pierce and...
notes: The verb stem '뚫다' means 'to pierce' or 'go through'. The '-고' ending is a connective that links two actions sequentially, meaning 'and then'. It implies the action in the next clause happens after this one.

meaning: Connects two clauses, indicating a sequence of actions. 'A-고 B' means 'do A and then do B'.

notes: '떼' means a group or swarm. '-들' is the plural marker. '-이' is the subject particle used after a consonant. The combination means 'the swarms'.

meaning: again

term: 몰라
type: Verb
breakdown: 모르다 + -아
meaning: don't know
notes: The informal, non-polite (반말, banmal) present tense of '모르다' (moreuda, to not know). This is a '르' irregular verb. When conjugated with '-아/어', the '으' is dropped and an extra 'ㄹ' is added to the previous syllable (모르 + 아 -> 몰라).
