=== [1:03:05] 진짜 너무 웃긴데 ===
meaning: She's really so funny, but...
tone: Informal, emphatic, leading to a contrast.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really
notes: A very common adverb in spoken Korean to add emphasis. It's equivalent to 'really' or 'truly'.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, very
notes: Traditionally, '너무' (neomu) meant 'too much' in a negative sense. However, in modern colloquial Korean, it's frequently used to mean 'very' or 'so' for both positive and negative emphasis, like in this sentence.
--- word ---
term: 웃긴데
type: Verb
breakdown: 웃기다 + -ㄴ데
meaning: is funny, but...
notes: The '-ㄴ/는데' (nde) ending is a versatile connective. Here, it provides background information ('she is funny') while implying a contrast or something else to follow ('...but...'). It's extremely common in conversation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Adjective/Verb Stem + -(으)ㄴ/는데
meaning: A connective ending used to provide background context for the next clause. It can imply a soft contrast ('but'), a reason ('so'), or simply connect two related ideas ('and').
example: 날씨가 좋은데 공원에 갈까요? (The weather is nice, so shall we go to the park?)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:03:07] 말도 잘해 ===

breakdown: 말 + 도
meaning: speech also / also speaks
notes: '말' (mal) means 'speech' or 'words'. The particle '-도' (do) means 'also' or 'too'. So, '말도' means 'also speech'.
--- word ---
term: 잘해

breakdown: 잘하다 -> 잘해
meaning: does well / is good at
notes: This is the informal present tense of '잘하다' (jalhada), which means 'to do well' or 'to be good at'. The phrase '말을 잘하다' (mareul jalhada) is a common idiom for being eloquent or a good speaker.

=== [1:03:12] 근데 ===

meaning: but, however
notes: '근데' (geunde) is the common spoken contraction of '그런데' (geureonde). It's used to introduce a contrasting idea ('but') or to change the subject ('by the way'). It's a staple of informal conversation.

=== [1:03:16] 그런 거 찍고 이런 거를 되게 무서워해 ===
meaning: She's really scared of things like filming and stuff.
tone: Informal, explanatory.
--- word ---
term: 그런
type: Determiner
breakdown: 그렇다 -> 그런
meaning: that kind of
notes: '그런' (geureon) comes from '그렇다' (geureota, 'to be so') and modifies a noun to mean 'that kind of' or 'such'. It refers to something mentioned or understood from context.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Noun
breakdown: 것 -> 거
meaning: thing, stuff
notes: '거' (geo) is the colloquial, spoken form of the pronoun '것' (geot), which means 'thing'. In conversation, '거' is used much more frequently than '것'.
--- word ---
term: 찍고
type: Verb
breakdown: 찍다 + -고
meaning: filming and...
notes: '찍다' (jjikda) means 'to film' or 'to take a photo'. The '-고' (go) ending connects it to the next part of the phrase, creating a list-like feeling: 'filming and this kind of stuff'.
--- word ---
term: 이런
type: Determiner
breakdown: 이렇다 -> 이런
meaning: this kind of
notes: Similar to '그런', '이런' (ireon) comes from '이렇다' (ireota, 'to be like this') and means 'this kind of' or 'this sort of'.
--- word ---
term: 거를
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 거 + 를
meaning: things (object)
notes: This is the colloquial form of '것을' (geoseul), where '거' (geo) means 'thing' and '-를' (reul) is the object particle, marking 'things' as the object of the verb '무서워해' (is scared of).
--- word ---
term: 되게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: very, really
notes: A colloquial adverb meaning 'very' or 'extremely'. It's often used in casual speech, similar in function to '아주' (aju), '매우' (maeu), or '진짜' (jinjja).
--- word ---
term: 무서워해
type: Verb
breakdown: 무서워하다 -> 무서워해
meaning: is scared of
notes: This is from '무서워하다' (museowohada), a verb describing the feeling of fear. It's formed from the adjective '무섭다' (museopda, 'to be scary') + the '-어하다' ending. This ending is typically used when describing the feelings or state of someone other than the speaker (i.e., you, he, she, they).
--- grammar ---
pattern: Adjective Stem + -아/어하다
meaning: Changes a descriptive verb (adjective) that describes a state or feeling into an action verb that describes the expression of that feeling. It's commonly used for third-person subjects.
example: 아이가 기뻐해요. (The child is happy/is acting happy.) vs. 나는 기뻐요. (I am happy.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:03:20] 그런데 어느 날 언니가 ===

--- word ---
term: 그런데
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: But, however
notes: Used to introduce a contrasting idea or to change the topic. In casual conversation, it's very often shortened to 근데 (geunde).
--- word ---
term: 어느
type: Determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: One, a certain
notes: Used before a noun to refer to an unspecified or particular item, person, or time. For example, '어느 날' (one day), '어느 나라' (which country).
--- word ---
term: 날
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Day
notes: A common noun for 'day'. Can be combined with other words like '오늘' (today) or '생일날' (birthday).
--- word ---
term: 언니가
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 언니 + -가
meaning: My older sister (as subject)
notes: 언니 (eonni) is used by a female to refer to her older sister or a close older female friend. The particle -가 marks it as the subject of the sentence. -가 is used after nouns ending in a vowel, while -이 is used after nouns ending in a consonant.

type: Noun + Particle (Contraction)
breakdown: 것 + -이
meaning: The thing (is)
notes: A common contraction of 것이 (geos-i). The pattern 'Verb-는 것' nominalizes a verb, turning 'to film' (찍다) into 'the act of filming' (찍는 것).
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: So, very, too
notes: While traditionally meaning 'too much' (often with a negative nuance), in modern colloquial Korean, it's frequently used simply to mean 'very' or 'so', similar to '아주' or '정말'.
--- word ---
term: 힘들어
type: Adjective
breakdown: 힘들다
meaning: is difficult/hard
notes: The informal, non-polite (반말, banmal) conjugation of 힘들다 (himdeulda). It expresses that something is physically or mentally taxing.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 것 / V-는 게
meaning: Turns a verb into a noun phrase, meaning 'the act of V-ing' or 'the thing that one Vs'. It allows a verb clause to function as a subject or object in a sentence.
example: 매일 운동하는 것은 건강에 좋아요. (Exercising every day is good for your health.)
level: Beginner

=== [1:03:29] 무서워 ===
meaning: It's scary.
tone: Informal, direct, expressing fear.
--- word ---
term: 무서워
type: Adjective
breakdown: 무섭다
meaning: is scary
notes: The informal, non-polite (반말, banmal) conjugation of 무섭다 (museopda). This is a 'ㅂ' irregular adjective. When conjugating with a vowel ending (like -아/어), the final 'ㅂ' changes to '우', so 무섭 + 어요 becomes 무서워요 (museowoyo).

=== [1:03:30] 이러고 막 언니가 그러다가 ===
meaning: My sister was saying things like that, and then...
tone: Informal, narrative, describing a past situation.
--- word ---
term: 이러고
type: Verb (Contraction)
breakdown: 이렇게 하고
meaning: Saying/doing this, and...
notes: A contraction of 이렇게 하고 (ireoke hago), meaning 'doing it like this and...'. It's often used colloquially to describe someone's actions or to quote what they said.
--- word ---
term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Just, kind of
notes: A very common colloquial adverb with many nuances. Here, it adds a sense of spontaneity or emphasis to the following action, like 'just' or 'all of a sudden'.
--- word ---
term: 언니가
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 언니 + -가
meaning: My older sister (as subject)
notes: 언니 (eonni) is used by a female for her older sister. The particle -가 marks it as the subject of the action.
--- word ---
term: 그러다가
type: Verb
breakdown: 그렇다 + -다가
meaning: While doing that, and then...
notes: The grammar pattern V-다가 indicates that an action was in progress when another action occurred, or that an action transitioned into another. Here, it means 'while she was saying that, then...'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-다가
meaning: Indicates a switch or transition from one action or state to another. The first action is interrupted or followed by the second.
example: 집에 가다가 친구를 만났어요. (On my way home, I met a friend.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:03:33] 근데 막내의 뭔가의 일들이라면 ===
meaning: But if it's something related to the youngest...
tone: Informal, hypothetical, explaining a reason.
--- word ---
term: 근데
type: Adverb (Contraction)
breakdown: 그런데
meaning: But, however
notes: A common contraction of 그런데 (geureonde) used in casual speech to connect contrasting ideas or change the subject.
--- word ---
term: 막내의
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 막내 + -의
meaning: The youngest's
notes: 막내 (maknae) refers to the youngest person in a family or group. The speaker is the maknae. The particle -의 is the possessive marker, like 's in English. In speech, it's often pronounced like '에' (e).
--- word ---
term: 뭔가의
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 무언가 + -의
meaning: Of something
notes: 뭔가 (mwonga) is a contraction of 무언가 (mueonga), meaning 'something'. Here, it adds a sense of vagueness, as in 'some kind of matter' or 'something's'.
--- word ---
term: 일들이라면
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 일들 + -(이)라면
meaning: If it's matters/things
notes: 일 (il) means 'matter' or 'work', and -들 (-deul) is the plural marker. The grammar pattern Noun + -(이)라면 expresses a hypothetical condition, meaning 'if it is [Noun]'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + -(이)라면
meaning: A conditional pattern used with nouns, meaning 'if it is/were [Noun]'. It's used to set up a hypothetical situation.
example: 학생이라면 할인을 받을 수 있어요. (If you are a student, you can get a discount.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:03:39] 그냥 다 노력을 해서라도 ===
meaning: even if it means making an effort,
tone: Informal, determined, explaining a mindset.
--- word ---
term: 그냥
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Just, simply
notes: A versatile adverb often meaning 'just because' or 'without any special reason'. Here it implies doing something without overthinking it, just going ahead and doing it.
--- word ---
term: 다
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: All, completely
notes: An adverb indicating that the action applies to everything or is done completely.
--- word ---
term: 노력을
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 노력 + -을
meaning: Effort (as object)
notes: 노력 (noryeok) means 'effort'. It's often paired with the verb 하다 (hada) to mean 'to make an effort' (노력하다). -을 is the object particle.
--- word ---
term: 해서라도
type: Verb + Particle
breakdown: 하다 + -아/어서 + -라도
meaning: Even if it means doing
notes: This combines V-아/어서 ('and so', 'by doing') with the particle -(이)라도 ('even if', 'at least'). The combined form V-아/어서라도 means 'even if I have to [V]' or 'by means of [V-ing], if nothing else'. It shows a strong will to achieve something despite obstacles.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어서라도
meaning: Expresses determination to do something, even if it requires a difficult or undesirable action. It means 'even if I have to V' or 'by V-ing at all costs'.
example: 밤을 새워서라도 숙제를 끝낼 거예요. (I'll finish the homework, even if I have to stay up all night.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:03:43] 뭔가 참여를 해주고 싶다고 ===
meaning: (she said) she wants to participate in some way.
tone: Informal, quoting, expressing a desire.
--- word ---
term: 뭔가
type: Adverb/Pronoun
breakdown: 무언가
meaning: Somehow, in some way
notes: A contraction of 무언가 (mueonga), 'something'. Here it's used adverbially to mean 'in some way' or 'somehow', adding a slight sense of vagueness.
--- word ---
term: 참여를
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 참여 + -를
meaning: Participation (as object)
notes: 참여 (chamyeo) means 'participation'. It is often used with 하다 (hada) to form the verb 참여하다 (chamyeohada), 'to participate'.
--- word ---
term: 해주고
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + -아/어 주다 + -고
meaning: Do for (me) and...
notes: This is the benefactive verb 해주다 (haejuda), which combines 하다 (to do) and 주다 (to give). It means the action is performed for someone else's benefit. Here, the sister wants to participate for the speaker's sake.
--- word ---
term: 싶다고
type: Verb Ending
breakdown: -고 싶다 + -고
meaning: (that she) wants to
notes: This combines two patterns. V-고 싶다 (go sipda) means 'to want to V'. The final -고 (go) is a quoting particle, a shortened form of -고 하다 (go hada), used to report what someone said. The full meaning is '...saying that she wants to'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고 싶다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's desire or wish to do something. It attaches to a verb stem.
example: 한국에 가고 싶어요. (I want to go to Korea.)
level: Beginner

=== [1:03:46] 뭐 이런 식으로 말을 하더라고 ===

tone: Informal, reporting, summarizing a conversation.
--- word ---
term: 뭐
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Well, like
notes: Often used as a filler word in casual speech, similar to 'like', 'well', or 'you know' in English. It can soften a statement or indicate slight hesitation.
--- word ---
term: 이런

breakdown: 이렇다 + -ㄴ
meaning: This kind of
notes: The adnominal form of 이렇다 (ireota, 'to be like this'). It modifies the following noun, 식 (sik).
--- word ---
term: 식으로

breakdown: 식 + -으로
meaning: In this way/manner
notes: 식 (sik) means 'style', 'way', or 'manner'. The particle -(으)로 indicates the means or method by which something is done.
--- word ---
term: 말을
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 말 + -을
meaning: Words (as object)

term: 하더라고

breakdown: 하다 + -더라고
meaning: She said / I recall her saying
notes: The retrospective ending -더라고 (deorago) is used when the speaker is reporting something they personally witnessed, heard, or felt in the past. It adds a nuance of recollection or mild surprise.
--- grammar ---

meaning: A retrospective ending used to state a fact the speaker learned through direct experience in the past. It conveys a sense of recalling or reporting a past observation.
example: 어제 그 식당에 갔는데 음식이 정말 맛있더라고요. (I went to that restaurant yesterday, and I found the food was really delicious.)

=== [1:04:00] 난 진짜 기억을 못 해 ===
meaning: I really can't remember.
tone: Informal, emphatic, slightly apologetic.
--- word ---
term: 난
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 + 는
meaning: I (as for me)
notes: A common contraction of 나는 (na-neun). 나 is the informal pronoun for 'I', and 는 is the topic marking particle.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really / truly
notes: A very common word used to add emphasis in casual speech. It can function as an adverb ('really') or a noun ('the real thing').
--- word ---
term: 기억을
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 기억 + 을
meaning: memory (as object)
notes: 기억 means 'memory'. The particle 을 marks it as the direct object of the verb. In casual conversation, this particle is often dropped (e.g., '진짜 기억 못 해').
--- word ---
term: 못
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: cannot
notes: This adverb is placed before a verb to indicate inability or impossibility. It's different from 안, which indicates a choice not to do something. '기억 못 해' (can't remember) vs. '기억 안 해' (don't remember, by choice).
--- word ---
term: 해
type: verb
breakdown: 하다
meaning: do
notes: The informal, non-polite (반말, banmal) conjugation of the verb 하다 (to do). Here it combines with 기억 to form 기억(을) 하다 (to remember).

=== [1:04:01] 언니들한테 물어보면 언니들이 ===
meaning: If I ask my sisters, my sisters...
tone: Informal, hypothetical, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 언니들한테
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 언니들 + 한테
meaning: to my older sisters
notes: 언니들 is the plural of 언니 (older sister). The particle -한테 is the colloquial equivalent of -에게, meaning 'to' a person or animal. It is primarily used in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 물어보면
type: verb
breakdown: 물어보다 + -(으)면
meaning: if I ask
notes: 물어보다 is a compound verb meaning 'to ask'. The -(으)면 ending attaches to a verb stem to create a conditional clause, meaning 'if' or 'when'.
--- word ---
term: 언니들이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 언니들 + 이
meaning: my older sisters (as subject)
notes: The particle -이 is the subject marker, used here because the noun 언니들 ends in a consonant. It indicates that the sisters are the ones who will perform the action in the next clause.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)면
meaning: Expresses a condition or hypothesis, meaning 'if' or 'when' something happens.
example: 시간이 있으면 영화 볼까요? (If we have time, shall we watch a movie?)
level: Beginner

=== [1:04:03] 알아서 착착착착 말할 텐데 ===
meaning: would just tell me everything smoothly, but...
tone: Informal, speculative, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 알아서
type: adverb
breakdown: 알다 + -아서
meaning: on one's own / automatically
notes: This expression means to do something using one's own judgment without being told. It implies the sisters would know exactly what funny stories to tell and just start talking.
--- word ---
term: 착착착착
type: adverb (mimetic)
breakdown: 
meaning: smoothly / in an orderly way
notes: This is a mimetic word (의태어) that describes something progressing smoothly and efficiently. Repeating it adds emphasis.
--- word ---
term: 말할
type: verb form
breakdown: 말하다 + -ㄹ
meaning: would speak
notes: The future/prospective modifier form of 말하다 (to speak). It modifies the implied noun in the -ㄹ 텐데 grammar pattern.
--- word ---
term: 텐데
type: verb ending
breakdown: -ㄹ/을 터 + 이다 + -ㄴ데
meaning: would..., but...
notes: The full form is -ㄹ/을 텐데. It expresses an expectation or assumption about a situation, often followed by a contrasting reality that is left unsaid. Here, the implied meaning is 'they would tell me, but they aren't here'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄹ 텐데
meaning: Expresses an expected or supposed situation, often with a nuance of contrast or regret. It's like saying 'I suppose/expect that..., but...'.
example: 지금 가면 길이 막힐 텐데. (If we go now, the traffic will probably be bad.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:04:06] 재밌는 거 진짜 많은데 ===

term: 재밌는

meaning: fun / interesting
notes: The present tense modifier form of the descriptive verb 재미있다 (to be fun). It modifies the following noun, 거.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: noun
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing / stuff
notes: A very common colloquial contraction of the noun 것 (geot), meaning 'thing'. In this context, '재밌는 거' can be translated as 'fun things' or more specifically 'fun stories'.
--- word ---
term: 진짜

breakdown: 
meaning: really / very
notes: An adverb used to add emphasis, similar to 'very' or 'so much'.
--- word ---
term: 많은데
type: adjective ending
breakdown: 많다 + -는데
meaning: there are many, but...
notes: The -는데 ending provides background information or sets up a contrast. By ending the sentence here, the speaker implies a contrast, like '...but I can't remember any right now'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: A/V-는데 / -(으)ㄴ데
meaning: A connective ending used to provide background context, show a soft contrast, or express surprise. It's similar to '..., and/so...' or '..., but...'.
example: 배고픈데 뭐 먹을까요? (I'm hungry, so what should we eat?)
level: Beginner

=== [1:04:14] 일단 지금 좀 졸려가지고 생각이 안 난다 ===
meaning: First of all, I'm a bit sleepy right now, so I can't think.
tone: Informal, explanatory, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 일단
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: first of all, for now
notes: Used to prioritize an action or state. It implies that other things will be considered later. It's very common in spoken Korean to start a sentence, similar to 'First,' or 'To begin with,'.
--- word ---
term: 지금
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now, right now
notes: Refers to the present moment. Often confused with 이제 (ije), which means 'now' but implies a change from a previous state (e.g., 'From now on, I will study'). 지금 (jigeum) simply points to the current time.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금
meaning: a bit, a little
notes: A common contraction of 조금 (jogeum). In conversation, 좀 (jom) is used far more frequently. It can also be used to soften a request, making it sound more polite, e.g., '물 좀 주세요' (mul jom juseyo) - 'Please give me some water'.
--- word ---
term: 졸려가지고
type: Verb connective
breakdown: 졸리다 + -아/어 가지고
meaning: because I'm sleepy
notes: The -아/어 가지고 (-a/eo gajigo) ending indicates a reason or cause for the following clause. It's primarily used in spoken Korean and is similar in meaning to -아/어서 (-a/eoseo). The action in the first clause is completed before the second clause begins.
--- word ---
term: 생각이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 생각 + -이
meaning: a thought (as subject)
notes: 생각 (saenggak) means 'thought' or 'idea'. The particle -이 (-i) marks it as the subject of the verb. The phrase '생각이 나다' (saenggagi nada) is a common idiom meaning 'to come to mind' or 'to remember'.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: This is a short-form negator placed directly before a verb or adjective. It's the simpler, more common way to negate in conversation compared to the long-form -지 않다 (-ji anta).
--- word ---
term: 난다
type: Verb
breakdown: 나다 + -ㄴ다
meaning: it comes out, it occurs
notes: The -ㄴ다/는다 (-neunda) ending is the plain form (or narrative form) for present tense action verbs. While it can be used in writing, in speech it often gives a sense of self-talk, observation, or a casual statement of fact, as used here.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 가지고
meaning: Indicates the reason or cause for the following clause. Common in spoken language.
example: 비가 와가지고 집에 있었어요. (biga wagajigo jibe isseosseoyo.) - It was raining, so I stayed home.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: 생각이 나다
meaning: An idiomatic expression meaning 'to remember' or 'to have an idea pop into one's head'. Literally 'a thought comes out'.
example: 갑자기 좋은 생각이 났어! (gapjagi joeun saenggagi nasseo!) - I just had a great idea!
level: Beginner

=== [1:04:17] 언니들 썰은 내가 생각나면 재밌는 걸 풀어주지 ===
meaning: As for stories about my sisters, if I think of a fun one, I'll tell it.
tone: Informal, promising, conversational.
--- word ---
term: 언니들
type: Noun
breakdown: 언니 + -들
meaning: older sisters
notes: 언니 (eonni) is used by a female speaker to refer to an older sister or a close older female friend. The suffix -들 (-deul) makes the noun plural. The speaker, Hyejin, is the youngest of three sisters.
--- word ---
term: 썰은
type: Noun (Slang) + Particle
breakdown: 썰 + -은
meaning: as for the story
notes: 썰 (sseol) is slang for 'story' or 'anecdote', derived from the word 설 (seol), meaning 'tale'. It's very common among younger generations. The particle -은/는 (-eun/neun) marks it as the topic of the sentence.
--- word ---
term: 내가
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + -가
meaning: I (as the subject)
notes: This is the informal way to say 'I'. 나 (na) is the pronoun, and -가 (-ga) is the subject marker. In formal speech, 저 (jeo) would be used instead of 나.
--- word ---
term: 생각나면
type: Verb
breakdown: 생각나다 + -(으)면
meaning: if it comes to mind
notes: The -(으)면 (-eumyeon) ending attaches to verb stems to mean 'if' or 'when'. 생각나다 (saenggangnada) is a compound verb from 생각 (thought) and 나다 (to emerge), meaning 'to remember'.
--- word ---
term: 재밌는
type: Adjective
breakdown: 재밌다 + -는
meaning: an interesting / fun (one)
notes: This is the descriptive form of the adjective 재밌다 (jaemitda), 'to be fun'. The -는 (-neun) ending modifies the following noun (in this case, '걸', which means 'thing').
--- word ---
term: 걸
type: Noun + Particle (Contraction)
breakdown: 것 + -을
meaning: the thing (as object)
notes: A very common contraction of 것을 (geoseul). 것 (geot) means 'thing' or 'the one', and -을 (-eul) is the object particle. In casual speech, contractions like this are the norm.
--- word ---
term: 풀어주지
type: Verb
breakdown: 풀다 + -아/어 주다 + -지
meaning: I'll tell it (for you)
notes: The idiom 썰을 풀다 (sseoreul pulda) means 'to tell a story'. The grammar -아/어 주다 (-a/eo juda) adds the nuance of doing something 'for someone'. The ending -지 (-ji) here is used in casual speech to state something with gentle certainty or as a promise.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 썰(을) 풀다
meaning: A colloquial/slang expression meaning 'to tell a story' or 'to spin a yarn'.
example: 어제 있었던 썰 풀어줄게. (eoje isseotdeon sseol pureojulge.) - I'll tell you a story about what happened yesterday.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 주다
meaning: Indicates that an action is performed for the benefit of someone else. It adds a sense of favor or service.
example: 문 좀 열어 주세요. (mun jom yeoreo juseyo.) - Please open the door for me.
level: Beginner

=== [1:04:24] 맞아 어렸을 때 나는 ===
meaning: That's right, when I was young, I...
tone: Informal, affirmative, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 맞아
type: Verb
breakdown: 맞다
meaning: That's right
notes: The informal, non-past tense conjugation of 맞다 (matda), meaning 'to be correct'. It's used frequently in conversation to agree with someone, equivalent to 'Yeah,' 'Right,' or 'Exactly'.
--- word ---
term: 어렸을
type: Adjective
breakdown: 어리다 + -었을
meaning: when (I) was young
notes: This is part of the '-(으)ㄹ 때' grammar pattern for past events. It's formed from the adjective 어리다 (eorida - to be young) + the past tense infix -었- (-eoss-) + the prospective modifier -(으)ㄹ (-eul).
--- word ---
term: 때
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: time, when
notes: A noun that means 'time' or 'moment'. It's very frequently used with modifying clauses to specify a time, as in '어렸을 때' (eoryeosseul ttae) - 'when I was young'.
--- word ---
term: 나는
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + -는
meaning: I, as for me
notes: 나 (na) is the informal pronoun for 'I'. The particle -는 (-neun) is the topic marker, indicating that the speaker is now going to talk about herself. The sentence trails off, leaving the listener to wait for the rest of the thought.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Adjective/Verb stem + -(으)ㄹ 때
meaning: A pattern used to mean 'when...' or 'at the time of...'. For past events, the past tense infix -았/었- is added before -(으)ㄹ 때.
example: 학생이었을 때 공부를 열심히 했어요. (haksaengieosseul ttae gongbureul yeolsimhi haesseoyo.) - I studied hard when I was a student.
level: Beginner

=== [1:04:31] 불담당 물담당 ===

tone: Informal, listing, reminiscent.

term: 불담당
type: Noun
breakdown: 불 + 담당
meaning: person in charge of fire
notes: A compound noun made of 불 (bul), meaning 'fire', and 담당 (damdang), meaning 'person in charge'. This likely refers to a childhood chore or role, perhaps during camping or cooking.
--- word ---
term: 물담당
type: Noun
breakdown: 물 + 담당
meaning: person in charge of water
notes: A compound noun made of 물 (mul), meaning 'water', and 담당 (damdang), meaning 'person in charge'. The speaker is listing roles she and her sisters had.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + 담당
meaning: A common construction to mean 'person in charge of [Noun]'. 담당 (damdang) can also be a verb, '담당하다' (damdanghada), meaning 'to be in charge of'.
example: 저는 마케팅 담당입니다. (jeoneun maketing damdangimnida.) - I am in charge of marketing.

breakdown: 
meaning: the most, number one
notes: Used to form the superlative (like 'most' or '-est' in English). It's interchangeable with 가장 (gajang), but 제일 (jeil) is often preferred in casual conversation.
--- word ---
term: 적고

breakdown: 적다 + -고
meaning: was small, and...

meaning: A connective particle that links two clauses. It can indicate a sequence of events ('and then') or simply list two or more facts or states ('and').
example: 밥을 먹고 영화를 봤어요. (babeul meokgo yeonghwareul bwasseoyo.) - I ate and then watched a movie.

=== [1:04:40] 진짜 8,000원 밖에 안 됐어 ===
meaning: It was really only 8,000 won.
tone: Informal, emphatic, reminiscent.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, for real
notes: A very common adverb used in casual speech to add emphasis, similar to 'really' or 'seriously'. It can also be used as a standalone exclamation: '진짜?!' (Jinjja?!) - 'Really?!'
--- word ---
term: 8,000원
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: 8,000 won
notes: 팔천 원 (palcheon won). Won (원) is the currency of South Korea. The speaker is emphasizing how small her allowance was.
--- word ---
term: 밖에
type: Particle
breakdown: 
meaning: only, nothing but
notes: This particle is always followed by a negative verb (like 안, 못, 없다). The combination 'Noun + 밖에 + negative' means 'only Noun' or 'nothing but Noun'. It often carries a nuance that the amount is less than expected or desired.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: The short-form negator. Here, it's a required part of the '밖에' grammar pattern.
--- word ---
term: 됐어
type: Verb
breakdown: 되다 + -었어
meaning: it was
notes: The informal past tense of 되다 (doeda), which means 'to become' or 'to be'. In this context, '안 됐어' (an dwaesseo) means 'it wasn't', completing the '밖에 안' pattern to mean 'it was only'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + 밖에 + Negative Verb
meaning: Expresses the idea of 'only' or 'nothing but'. It emphasizes the smallness or scarcity of something.
example: 냉장고에 우유밖에 없어요. (naengjanggo-e uyubakke eopseoyo.) - There is only milk in the fridge.
level: Intermediate

=== [1:04:42] 초등학교 때 ===

notes: When attached directly to a time-related noun like 초등학교 (chodeunghakgyo), it means 'during that period' or 'at that time'. For example, '방학 때' (banghak ttae) means 'during school vacation'.

meaning: Indicates the time period or moment when something happens. It means 'during [Noun]' or 'at the time of [Noun]'.
example: 점심때 뭐 먹을까? (jeomsimttae mwo meogeulkka?) - What should we eat at lunchtime?

=== [1:04:43] 심하지 않았어? ===
meaning: Wasn't that harsh?
tone: Informal, rhetorical question, seeking agreement.
--- word ---
term: 심하지
type: verb stem + connector
breakdown: 심하다 + -지
meaning: to be severe/harsh and...
notes: 심하다 means 'to be severe, extreme, or harsh'. The ending -지 is used here as part of the '-지 않다' negative construction. It connects the verb stem to the negative auxiliary verb.
--- word ---
term: 않았어?
type: auxiliary verb ending
breakdown: 않다 + -았- + -어?
meaning: wasn't it?
notes: This is the past tense informal question form of 않다 (to not be). -았- is the past tense marker. -어? is a standard informal (반말) question ending. The whole pattern 'V-지 않았어?' means 'Didn't V?' or 'Wasn't it V?', often used to confirm something the speaker believes is true.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb/Adjective + -지 않다
meaning: A common way to negate a verb or adjective, meaning 'to not do' or 'to not be'. It can feel slightly softer or more descriptive than the alternative '안 + Verb' negation.
example: 춥지 않아요. (It's not cold.)
level: Beginner

notes: The particle -(으)로 indicates the means, tool, or method by which an action is performed. Since 원 ends in a consonant, -으로 is used. If the noun ended in a vowel, -로 would be used (e.g., 차로 - by car).

term: 200불

breakdown: 
meaning: 200 dollars

type: noun + particle
breakdown: 칠 + -을
meaning: the act of paying
notes: This is an idiosyncratic or colloquial usage. It's likely a personalized noun form related to 지불 (payment) or 치르다 (to pay). The speaker is using it to mean 'the payment' or 'the deal'. -을 is the object particle.
--- word ---
term: 하겠어?
type: verb ending
breakdown: 하다 + -겠- + -어?
meaning: do you think I would do?
notes: The infix -겠- here doesn't indicate the future. In a question, it often expresses doubt, conjecture, or a strong rhetorical feeling, like 'Do you really think...?' or 'There's no way...'. It makes the question sound incredulous.

=== [1:04:49] 못 하지 ===
meaning: Of course you can't.
tone: Informal, stating an obvious fact.
--- word ---
term: 못
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: cannot
notes: 못 is used before a verb to express inability ('cannot'), as opposed to 안 which expresses unwillingness ('do not'). For example, '수영 못 해요' (I can't swim) vs. '수영 안 해요' (I don't swim).
--- word ---
term: 하지
type: verb ending
breakdown: 하다 + -지
meaning: do (of course)
notes: When used as a sentence ender in informal speech (반말), -지 confirms a fact that the speaker and listener likely both know. It carries a nuance of 'of course', 'right?', or 'as you know'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 못 + Verb
meaning: Expresses that the subject lacks the ability or that it is physically impossible to perform the action. It's placed directly before the verb.
example: 너무 바빠서 못 가요. (I'm too busy, so I can't go.)
level: Beginner

=== [1:04:51] 그러니까 언니들한테 바칠 돈이 없는 거야 ===

--- word ---
term: 그러니까
type: conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: so / therefore / that's why
notes: A very common connector used to state a reason or conclusion based on the previous statement. It's often used at the beginning of a sentence to mean 'So... ' or 'That's why...'.
--- word ---
term: 언니들한테
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 언니 + -들 + -한테
meaning: to the older sisters
notes: 언니 is what a female calls her older sister or a close older female friend. -들 is the plural marker. -한테 is the informal particle meaning 'to' or 'from' someone. Its formal equivalent is -에게.
--- word ---
term: 바칠
type: verb (future modifier form)
breakdown: 바치다 + -ㄹ
meaning: (that I would) offer
notes: 바치다 means 'to offer' or 'to dedicate', often to someone of higher status. The -(으)ㄹ ending turns the verb into an adjective that modifies the following noun (돈), indicating a future or potential action: 'money to offer'.
--- word ---
term: 돈이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 돈 + -이
meaning: money (as subject)
notes: 돈 means 'money'. -이 is the subject particle, used here because the sentence is about the existence (or lack thereof) of money.
--- word ---
term: 없는
type: adjective (present modifier form)
breakdown: 없다 + -는
meaning: that doesn't exist
notes: 없다 means 'to not exist'. The -는 ending turns it into an adjective that modifies the following noun phrase (거야), describing a current state.
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: noun + verb ending (colloquial)
breakdown: 것 + -이야
meaning: it is that...
notes: This is a very common informal sentence ending. It's a contraction of 것 (thing, fact) + 이야 (is). The pattern '-는 거야' is used to explain a situation or state a fact with a sense of emphasis or realization.

=== [1:05:10] 갑자기 이렇게 졸릴 수가 있나? ===

tone: Informal, rhetorical question expressing surprise.

term: 갑자기

meaning: suddenly

--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + -게
meaning: like this, this much
notes: A contraction of 이렇하게. It's used to indicate the manner or degree of an action or state. It often translates to 'so' or 'this' when modifying an adjective, e.g., 이렇게 예뻐요 (It's so pretty).

meaning: possibility that...
notes: 수 is a dependent noun meaning 'way' or 'possibility'. It's almost always used in the pattern -(으)ㄹ 수 있다/없다 (can/cannot). Here, -가 is the subject particle.
--- word ---

meaning: is it possible?
notes: The -나? ending is an informal question form used to express doubt, wonder, or a question to oneself. It's softer and more introspective than a standard -어/아? question. For example, '비가 오나?' (I wonder if it's raining?).

meaning: Expresses ability or possibility ('can' / 'cannot').

term: 이거
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 이것
meaning: this
notes: 이거 is the colloquial, spoken form of 이것 (this thing). It's very common in casual conversation.

term: 아무래도
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: it seems, after all
notes: This adverb is used when making a judgment or guess after considering the circumstances. It often translates to 'I think', 'it seems', 'after all', or 'no matter how I look at it'.
--- word ---
term: 혈스인
type: Noun + Copula (adnominal form)
breakdown: 혈스 + 이다 + -ㄴ
meaning: that is a blood sugar spike
notes: 혈스 is a slang abbreviation for 혈당 스파이크 (blood sugar spike). 이다 is the copula ('to be'). The ending -ㄴ turns the 'Noun + 이다' phrase into a form that can modify the next noun, 거 (thing).
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Noun (dependent)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing, fact
notes: 거 is the colloquial form of the dependent noun 것 (thing, fact). It's used here to nominalize the preceding clause.
--- word ---
term: 같아
type: Adjective
breakdown: 같다 + -아
meaning: it seems like
notes: The informal, non-polite form of 같다 (to be like, to seem). Used to express a guess or opinion. The full pattern is Noun + 인 것 같다.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + -인 것 같다
meaning: Expresses a guess or opinion that something 'seems to be' a certain noun.
example: 저 사람은 학생인 것 같아요. (That person seems to be a student.)

meaning: my

meaning: Dad

--- grammar ---

meaning: Used to provide background context about a noun in the past before continuing with a related or contrasting statement. Translates to 'It was [Noun], and/but...'

=== [1:06:39] 아버님들은 왜 그러셨을까? ===

--- word ---
term: 왜

--- grammar ---

notes: In K-pop culture, '시즌 그리팅' (Season's Greetings) is a well-known term for an artist's yearly merchandise box. Hyejin is repeating the topic, perhaps while reading a fan's comment about it.

=== [1:07:10] 내년에 ===
meaning: Next year...

term: 내년에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 내년 (next year) + -에 (time/place particle)

=== [1:07:13] 내년에 니가 모으게 ===
meaning: So that you (can) collect them next year.
tone: Informal, declarative, directed at a fan.
--- word ---
term: 내년에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 내년 (next year) + -에 (time/place particle)
meaning: next year
notes: The particle -에 marks a point in time. Here, it sets the timeframe for the action that follows.
--- word ---
term: 니가
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 네 (you) + -가 (subject particle)
meaning: you
notes: 니가 is the common spoken form of 네가 (you + subject particle). Speakers say '니가' to avoid confusion with 내가 (I + subject particle), as 네 and 내 can sound very similar. While 네가 is grammatically correct in writing, 니가 is overwhelmingly used in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 모으게
type: verb ending
breakdown: 모으다 (to collect) + -게 (adverbial ending)
meaning: so that (you) collect
notes: The -게 ending has several uses. Here, it indicates purpose or result, similar to 'so that...' or 'in a way that...'. The full sentence might be something like '내년에 니가 모으게 (만들게)' which means '(I'll make it) so that you collect them next year,' implying she will release something good.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -게
meaning: An adverbial ending that can indicate purpose ('so that...'), result ('in a way that...'), or be part of the V + -게 하다 ('to make someone do V') pattern.
example: 잘 보이게 크게 써 주세요. (Please write it big so that I can see it well.)
level: Intermediate

meaning: already

meaning: rather than (me recalling that they were) watching

meaning: Used to express a preference for one action or state over another, meaning 'rather than A, B is better/true'.

=== [1:08:51] 정신을 못 차리겠어 ===

--- word ---
term: 정신을
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 정신 + -을

--- word ---

type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: cannot
notes: 못 is an adverb used before a verb to express inability ('cannot'). It's different from 안, which expresses unwillingness ('do not'). For example, '수영 못 해요' (I can't swim) vs. '수영 안 해요' (I don't swim).

term: 차리겠어

breakdown: 차리다 + -겠- + -어
meaning: can pull together
notes: The base verb is 차리다 (to set up, to regain). The full idiom is 정신을 차리다 (to pull oneself together). The infix -겠- here adds a nuance of strong feeling or conjecture about the current state, emphasizing the difficulty.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 정신을 차리다
meaning: An idiom meaning 'to pull oneself together,' 'to come to one's senses,' or 'to snap out of it.'
example: 시험 전날이라 정신을 차리고 공부해야 돼. (It's the day before the exam, so I have to get serious and study.)

=== [1:09:00] 먹지 말았어야 했나 봐 ===

tone: Informal, regretful, speculative.
--- word ---
term: 먹지
type: Verb
breakdown: 먹다 + -지
meaning: eat
notes: The verb stem 먹다 (to eat) combined with the particle -지, which is part of the negative command/suggestion form V-지 말다 (to not do V).
--- word ---
term: 말았어야 했나 봐
type: Grammatical Construction
breakdown: 말다 + -았어야 했다 + -나 보다
meaning: I guess I shouldn't have
notes: This combines two patterns. V-지 말았어야 했다 means 'should not have done V', expressing regret. -(으)ㄴ/나 보다 means 'it seems' or 'I guess', expressing speculation. Together, it forms a speculative regret.

meaning: Expresses regret for an action that was done; 'should not have done (verb)'.
example: 그 말을 하지 말았어야 했어. (I shouldn't have said that.)

--- grammar ---
pattern: V-나 보다
meaning: Expresses a guess or assumption based on some evidence; 'it seems that...', 'I guess...'. Used with verbs.
example: 밖에 비가 오나 봐요. (It seems like it's raining outside.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:09:16] 왜 오래 버텨야 돼? ===

meaning: must endure

meaning: have to?

meaning: Expresses obligation or necessity, equivalent to 'must' or 'have to' in English.

=== [1:09:30] 가기 전에 ===
meaning: before I go...
tone: Informal, connecting phrase.
--- word ---
term: 가기
type: Noun form of verb
breakdown: 가다 + -기
meaning: going
notes: The suffix '-기' is attached to a verb stem to turn it into a noun, a process called nominalization. It's similar to adding '-ing' in English (e.g., 'go' -> 'going'). This form is required to use the verb with the particle '전에' (jeone).
--- word ---
term: 전에
type: Postpositional particle
breakdown: 전 + 에
meaning: before
notes: This particle combination means 'before'. It follows a noun or a nominalized verb (a verb ending in '-기'). For example, '식사 전에' (siksa jeone) means 'before the meal'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-기 전에
meaning: Indicates an action that occurs before another action. It translates to 'before doing (verb)'.
example: 자기 전에 책을 읽어요. (Jagi jeone chaegeul ilgeoyo.) - I read a book before sleeping.
level: Beginner

=== [1:09:34] 내가 좋아하는 카페가 하나 있거든? ===
meaning: There's this one cafe I like, you know?
tone: Informal, sharing information with a friendly, conspiratorial tone.
--- word ---
term: 내가
type: Pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 + -가
meaning: I (as the subject)
notes: This is the informal way to say 'I' as the subject of a sentence. '나' (na) is the informal pronoun for 'I', and '-가' (ga) is the subject marker. When they combine, it becomes '내가' (naega). The formal equivalent is '제가' (jega).
--- word ---
term: 좋아하는
type: Verb (adjective form)
breakdown: 좋아하다 + -는
meaning: that (I) like
notes: This is a present tense relative clause. The ending '-는' attaches to an action verb stem to modify the following noun. Here, '좋아하는' modifies '카페' (cafe), creating the phrase 'the cafe that I like'.
--- word ---
term: 카페가
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 카페 + -가
meaning: a cafe (is)
notes: '-가' (ga) is the subject marking particle, used here because '카페' (cafe) is the subject of the verb '있다' (itda) - 'a cafe exists'.
--- word ---
term: 하나
type: Numeral
breakdown: 
meaning: one
notes: This is the native Korean number for 'one', used for counting things. It's often used to mean 'one thing' or 'a single'.
--- word ---
term: 있거든?
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 있다 + -거든
meaning: there is, you see?
notes: The '-거든(요)' ending is used in conversation to provide a reason or background information for what's being said. Here, the speaker uses it with a rising intonation ('-거든?') to share a piece of information and engage the listener, similar to saying '...you know?' or '...you see'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-거든(요)
meaning: A sentence ending used to provide a reason or background context for a statement that will follow or has just been made. In banmal (informal speech), the '-요' is dropped.
example: 밖에 비가 오거든. (Bakke biga ogeodeun.) - It's raining outside, so...
level: Intermediate

=== [1:09:37] 이름은 말해줄 수 없어 ===
meaning: I can't tell you the name.
tone: Informal, apologetic, slightly secretive.
--- word ---
term: 이름은
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 이름 + -은
meaning: The name (as for the name)
notes: '-은/는' is the topic marking particle. Using '은' here adds a nuance of contrast. The speaker is implying, 'I can tell you about the cafe, but as for the name... that I can't tell you'.
--- word ---
term: 말해줄
type: Verb
breakdown: 말하다 + -아/어 주다 + -ㄹ
meaning: (that I can) tell you
notes: This combines '말하다' (to speak/tell) and '-아/어 주다' (to do something for someone). '말해주다' means 'to tell someone (as a favor)'. The '-ㄹ' ending turns it into a future adnominal form that modifies '수' (way/ability).
--- word ---
term: 수
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: way, ability
notes: A bound noun that means 'way' or 'ability'. It is almost exclusively used in the patterns '-(으)ㄹ 수 있다' (can do) and '-(으)ㄹ 수 없다' (cannot do).
--- word ---
term: 없어
type: Adjective
breakdown: 없다 + -어
meaning: there isn't / to not have
notes: The informal, non-polite form of '없다' (eopda). In this context, combined with '-(으)ㄹ 수', it means 'cannot'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄹ 수 없다
meaning: Expresses inability or impossibility. It literally means 'the ability/way to do (verb) does not exist'. The opposite is 'V-(으)ㄹ 수 있다' (can do).
example: 지금은 운전할 수 없어요. (Jigeumeun unjeonhal su eopseoyo.) - I can't drive right now.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 주다
meaning: Indicates that an action is performed for the benefit of someone else. It adds a sense of favor or kindness.
example: 문을 열어 주세요. (Muneul yeoreo juseyo.) - Please open the door for me.
level: Beginner

=== [1:09:40] 미안하다 ===
meaning: Sorry.
tone: Informal, direct apology.
--- word ---
term: 미안하다
type: Adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: to be sorry
